    python3 gpr_view.py [--out /tmp/gpr_view.png] [--image some_frame.png]
"""
import argparse
import csv
import json
import os
import sys
from datetime import datetime

import cv2
import numpy as np

DUAL = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
sys.path.insert(0, DUAL)
import capture_gpr as c  # noqa: E402

TILE = 216          # px per half-tile (photo / mask)
CROP_PAD = 1.35     # crop = pot circle diameter x this


def todays_rows():
    """{pot_id: raw_gpr} from today's noon row, if it exists."""
    out = {}
    try:
        with open(c.OUT_CSV, newline="") as f:
            for r in csv.DictReader(f):
                if r["timestamp"].startswith(datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d")):
                    out[r["pot_id"]] = r["raw_gpr"]
    except (OSError, KeyError):
        pass
    return out


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="/tmp/gpr_view.png")
    ap.add_argument("--image", help="use a saved frame instead of the camera")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    cfg = json.load(open(c.ROI_FILE))
    rois, circular = cfg["rois"], bool(cfg.get("circular", True))
    if args.image:
        frame = cv2.imread(args.image)
    else:
        frame = c.capture_frame(use_light=c.USE_CAPTURE_LIGHT)
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(frame, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
    counted = c.green_mask(hsv)                    # what GPR counts
    raw = c.hsv_mask(hsv)                          # before the 3x3 opening
    removed = cv2.bitwise_and(raw, cv2.bitwise_not(counted))
    noon = todays_rows()

    # ---- top: whole bed ----
    top = frame.copy()
    top[removed > 0] = (0, 255, 255)               # yellow: speckle the opening removed
    top[counted > 0] = (255, 0, 255)               # magenta: counted as plant
    stats = {}
    for pid, roi in sorted(rois.items()):
        cx, cy, r = c.roi_geometry(roi)
        area = c.roi_mask(frame.shape[:2], roi, circular)
        g, tot = c.masked_count(counted, area), int(np.count_nonzero(area))
        stats[pid] = (g, tot, g / tot * 100 if tot else 0.0)
        cv2.circle(top, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(r), (255, 255, 0), 2)
        cv2.putText(top, f"{pid} {stats[pid][2]:.2f}%", (int(cx - r), int(cy - r) - 8),
                    cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.8, (255, 255, 0), 2)
    W = top.shape[1]
    hdr = np.zeros((70, W, 3), np.uint8)
    cv2.putText(hdr, f"GPR view  {datetime.now():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}   D={c.day_index(True)}   "
                f"exposure locked, same ROIs + mask as the noon capture",
                (12, 30), cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.75, (255, 255, 255), 2)
    cv2.putText(hdr, "MAGENTA = counted as plant    YELLOW = specks removed by 3x3 opening    "
                "CYAN = pot ROI", (12, 58), cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.6, (200, 200, 200), 1)

    # ---- bottom: per-pot close-ups in bed layout (rows by y, columns by x) ----
    order = sorted(rois, key=lambda p: rois[p]["y"])
    rows = [sorted(order[i:i + 3], key=lambda p: rois[p]["x"]) for i in range(0, 9, 3)]
    tile_w, tile_h = W // 3, TILE + 34
    grid = np.full((tile_h * 3, W, 3), 25, np.uint8)
    for ri, row in enumerate(rows):
        for ci, pid in enumerate(row):
            cx, cy, r = c.roi_geometry(rois[pid])
            half = int(r * CROP_PAD)
            x0, y0 = max(0, int(cx - half)), max(0, int(cy - half))
            x1, y1 = min(W, int(cx + half)), min(frame.shape[0], int(cy + half))
            photo = cv2.resize(frame[y0:y1, x0:x1], (TILE, TILE), interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)
            mk = frame[y0:y1, x0:x1].copy() // 3                       # dimmed background
            area = c.roi_mask(frame.shape[:2], rois[pid], circular)[y0:y1, x0:x1]
            mk[(counted[y0:y1, x0:x1] > 0) & (area > 0)] = (255, 0, 255)
            mk[(removed[y0:y1, x0:x1] > 0) & (area > 0)] = (0, 255, 255)
            mk = cv2.resize(mk, (TILE, TILE), interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)
            sc = TILE / (x1 - x0)
            cv2.circle(mk, (int((cx - x0) * sc), int((cy - y0) * sc)), int(r * sc), (255, 255, 0), 1)
            ox, oy = ci * tile_w + (tile_w - 2 * TILE) // 2, ri * tile_h + 30
            grid[oy:oy + TILE, ox:ox + TILE] = photo
            grid[oy:oy + TILE, ox + TILE:ox + 2 * TILE] = mk
            g, tot, gpr = stats[pid]
            label = f"{pid}  now {gpr:.2f}%  ({g} px)"
            if pid in noon:
                label += f"   noon {noon[pid]}%"
            cv2.putText(grid, label, (ox, oy - 9), cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.5, (255, 255, 255), 1)

    out = np.vstack([hdr, top, grid])
    cv2.imwrite(args.out, out)
    print(f"[gpr_view] {args.out}")
    for pid in sorted(stats):
        g, tot, gpr = stats[pid]
        print(f"  {pid}: now {gpr:5.2f}%  ({g:4d}/{tot} px)" + (f"   noon {noon[pid]}%" if pid in noon else ""))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\init_csvs.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""init_csvs.py -- create any missing data CSV with just its header row.

Headers come from the scripts that write the files, so they can't drift.
Existing files are never touched.
"""
import csv
import os
import sys

DUAL = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
sys.path.insert(0, DUAL)

import pi_link  # noqa: E402

# capture_gpr imports cv2/numpy at module level; read its header without them.
ns = {}
src = open(os.path.join(DUAL, "capture_gpr.py")).read()
start = src.index("CSV_HEADER = [")
exec(src[start:src.index("]", start) + 1], ns)

files = [
    (os.path.join(DUAL, "growth_dataset.csv"), ns["CSV_HEADER"]),
    (pi_link.SM_CSV, pi_link.SM_HEADER),
    (pi_link.EVT_CSV, pi_link.EVT_HEADER),
    (pi_link.ERR_CSV, pi_link.ERR_HEADER),
]
for path, header in files:
    if os.path.exists(path):
        print(f"  kept     {path}")
        continue
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", newline="") as f:
        csv.writer(f).writerow(header)
    print(f"  created  {path}")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\install_stability.sh ===

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# install_stability.sh -- one-time Pi settings for unattended running (safe to re-run).
#   * Wi-Fi: never give up auto-connecting (NetworkManager stops after 4 failures by
#     default -- the hotspot was then ignored until a reboot), Wi-Fi power saving off
#   * wifi-watchdog.timer: every 2 min, rescan + reconnect if not on Wi-Fi
#   * persistent journal (50 MB cap): logs survive reboots, so outages can be diagnosed
#   * fake-hwclock saved every 10 min instead of hourly: after a power loss the clock
#     restarts at most ~10 min + the outage behind, not up to an hour more
set -euo pipefail
TOOLS="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"

for c in $(nmcli -t -f NAME,TYPE connection show | awk -F: '$2=="802-11-wireless"{print $1}'); do
  sudo nmcli connection modify "$c" connection.autoconnect-retries 0 802-11-wireless.powersave 2
done
printf "[connection]\nwifi.powersave = 2\n" | sudo tee /etc/NetworkManager/conf.d/wifi-powersave-off.conf >/dev/null

sudo install -m 755 "$TOOLS/wifi_watchdog.sh" /usr/local/bin/wifi_watchdog.sh
sudo tee /etc/systemd/system/wifi-watchdog.service >/dev/null <<'U'
[Unit]
Description=Reconnect Wi-Fi (hotspot) if disconnected
After=NetworkManager.service
[Service]
Type=oneshot
ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/wifi_watchdog.sh
U
sudo tee /etc/systemd/system/wifi-watchdog.timer >/dev/null <<'U'
[Unit]
Description=Check Wi-Fi every 2 minutes
[Timer]
OnBootSec=2min
OnUnitActiveSec=2min
[Install]
WantedBy=timers.target
U

sudo mkdir -p /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d /var/log/journal
printf "[Journal]\nStorage=persistent\nSystemMaxUse=50M\n" | sudo tee /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/persist.conf >/dev/null

echo "*/10 * * * * root /usr/sbin/fake-hwclock save" | sudo tee /etc/cron.d/fake-hwclock-10min >/dev/null

sudo systemctl daemon-reload
sudo systemctl enable --now wifi-watchdog.timer
sudo systemctl restart systemd-journald
echo "stability settings installed"


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\light_log.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
light_log.py -- hourly light reading from the GPR camera (no extra hardware).

The camera runs with locked gain and is brightness-matched to the same target as
the noon capture. The exposure it needs is therefore a light meter: a darker sky
needs a longer exposure.

    light_index = (mean_v / target_v) * (1000 / exposure_ms)

The index is relative, not lux: 100 = the brightness of a 10 ms exposure on
target. It is only comparable between readings from this camera at this mount.
A reading at the 1 ms floor that is still too bright is marked "bright_clip",
and one at the 100 ms ceiling that is still too dark is marked "dark_clip".

Cron (installed by agrisentry.sh start). Skips 12:xx so it never fights the
noon capture for the camera:
    30 6-11,13-18 * * *  python3 ~/THESIS/Dual_Archi/tools/light_log.py

Output: ~/thesis/light_log.csv
    timestamp, day, exposure_us, mean_v, target_v, light_index, note
"""
import csv
import os
import sys
import time
from datetime import datetime

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(HERE))
import capture_gpr as cg  # noqa: E402

OUT = os.path.join(cg.BASE, "light_log.csv")
HEADER = ["timestamp", "day", "exposure_us", "mean_v", "target_v", "light_index", "note"]


def day_or_blank():
    try:
        d = (datetime.now().date() - datetime.strptime(cg.sowing_date(), "%Y-%m-%d").date()).days + 1
        return d if 1 <= d <= cg.CYCLE_DAYS else ""
    except ValueError:
        return ""


def main():
    stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
    note = ""
    try:
        cam = cg.open_locked_camera()
        time.sleep(2)
        cg._match_brightness(cam, cam.capture_array())
        cam.stop()
        cam.close()
        exp = cg.LAST_EXPOSURE["exposure_us"]
        v = cg.LAST_EXPOSURE["mean_v"]
        if exp <= cg.EXPOSURE_MIN_US and v > 1.05 * cg.TARGET_MEAN_V:
            note = "bright_clip"
        elif exp >= cg.EXPOSURE_MAX_US and v < 0.95 * cg.TARGET_MEAN_V:
            note = "dark_clip"
        idx = f"{(v / cg.TARGET_MEAN_V) * (1000.0 / (exp / 1000.0)):.1f}"
    except Exception as e:          # camera busy, not connected, ...
        exp, v, idx, note = "", "", "", f"error: {e}"[:120]

    new = not os.path.exists(OUT)
    with open(OUT, "a", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f)
        if new:
            w.writerow(HEADER)
        w.writerow([stamp, day_or_blank(), exp, v, cg.TARGET_MEAN_V, idx, note])
    print(f"[light_log] {stamp} exposure={exp} us mean_v={v} index={idx} {note}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\map_probes.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""map_probes.py -- find which channel each physical probe is wired to.

Takes a 5 s baseline, then reports every time a channel moves more than
--thresh counts away from its baseline (probe pulled into air or gripped)
and again when it settles back. Pull one probe at a time, in a known order.
Stops pi-link; restart it afterwards: sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link
"""
import argparse
import statistics
import time

from esp_link import EspLink, stop_daemon

ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
ap.add_argument("--secs", type=float, default=240)
ap.add_argument("--thresh", type=int, default=150)
args = ap.parse_args()


def read(esp):
    r = esp.ask("GET_RAW", "RAW", 8)
    if r and r.startswith("RAW"):
        return [int(v) for v in r.split(",")[2:]]
    return None


stop_daemon()
with EspLink() as esp:
    samples = []
    t0 = time.time()
    while time.time() - t0 < 5:
        v = read(esp)
        if v:
            samples.append(v)
        time.sleep(0.5)
    base = [int(statistics.median(s[i] for s in samples)) for i in range(9)]
    print("baseline " + " ".join(f"P-0{i+1}={b}" for i, b in enumerate(base)), flush=True)
    print("READY -- pull probes now", flush=True)
    moved = [False] * 9
    t_end = time.time() + args.secs
    while time.time() < t_end:
        v = read(esp)
        if not v:
            continue
        for i in range(9):
            d = v[i] - base[i]
            if not moved[i] and abs(d) > args.thresh:
                moved[i] = True
                print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S") + f"  MOVED   P-0{i+1}  {base[i]} -> {v[i]} ({d:+d})", flush=True)
            elif moved[i] and abs(d) < args.thresh / 2:
                moved[i] = False
                print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S") + f"  back    P-0{i+1}  {v[i]}", flush=True)
        time.sleep(0.4)
print("done. pi-link is STOPPED. Restart: sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link")

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# panel.sh -- one-screen summary of the running system, for the panel checking.
#   On the Pi:   ~/THESIS/system.sh panel     (or: bash ~/thesis/tools/panel.sh)
#   From Mac:    ./system.sh panel
# Read-only: it only reads the CSV files and service state.
DUAL="$HOME/THESIS/Dual_Archi"
DATA="$HOME/agrisentry_data"
B=$'\e[1m'; N=$'\e[0m'
rows() { [[ -f $1 ]] && echo $(( $(wc -l < "$1") - 1 )) || echo 0; }

echo "${B}AgriSentry -- system summary  $(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M')${N}"
python3 - "$DUAL/sowing_date.txt" <<'EOF'
import sys, datetime as dt
s = open(sys.argv[1]).read().strip()
d = (dt.date.today() - dt.date.fromisoformat(s)).days + 1
print(f"Sown {s}  ->  today is D{d} of 30  (harvest D30 = {dt.date.fromisoformat(s) + dt.timedelta(days=29)})")
EOF
mode="GERMINATION (all pots kept at 50-60 %)"
grep -q "treatment bands ON" "$DUAL/field_log.csv" 2>/dev/null && mode="TREATMENTS (low 30-40 / optimal 50-60 / high 70-80 %)"
echo "Watering mode : $mode"
echo "Logger        : $(systemctl is-active pi-link)   Noon capture: $(crontab -l 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^[^#].*capture_gpr.py' && echo ON || echo OFF)   Light log: $(crontab -l 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^[^#].*light_log.py' && echo ON || echo OFF)"
echo "Clock synced  : $(timedatectl show -p NTPSynchronized --value)"

echo; echo "${B}1. Soil moisture (latest, logged every 30 min) and automatic watering${N}"
python3 - "$DATA/sm_log.csv" "$DATA/irrigation_log.csv" "$mode" <<'EOF'
import csv, sys, os
sm = list(csv.DictReader(open(sys.argv[1]))) if os.path.exists(sys.argv[1]) else []
irr = list(csv.DictReader(open(sys.argv[2]))) if os.path.exists(sys.argv[2]) else []
germ = sys.argv[3].startswith("GERM")
grp = {1: "Low", 2: "Low", 3: "Low", 4: "Optimal", 5: "Optimal", 6: "Optimal", 7: "High", 8: "High", 9: "High"}
bnd = {"Low": (30, 40), "Optimal": (50, 60), "High": (70, 80)}
if not sm:
    print("  no readings yet"); sys.exit()
r = sm[-1]
print(f"  reading at {r['wall_time'].replace('T', ' ')[:16]}")
print(f"  {'pot':5} {'group':8} {'moisture':>9}  {'target':>7}  {'status':16} {'doses':>5} {'mL':>6}")
for i in range(1, 10):
    p = f"P-0{i}"; lo, hi = (50, 60) if germ else bnd[grp[i]]
    v = r.get(p, "NaN")
    st = "no reading" if v in ("", "NaN") else ("below -> waters" if float(v) < lo else "above" if float(v) > hi else "in band")
    ev = [e for e in irr if e["pot_id"] == p and e.get("status", "DOSED") == "DOSED"]
    ml = sum(float(e["dose_ml"] or 0) for e in ev)
    print(f"  {p:5} {grp[i]:8} {v:>8}%  {lo:>3}-{hi:<3}  {st:16} {len(ev):>5} {ml:>6.0f}")
print(f"  moisture readings logged: {len(sm)}   automatic doses: {len(irr)}")
EOF

echo; echo "${B}2. Daily noon capture -- growth dataset${N}"
python3 - "$DUAL/growth_dataset.csv" "$DUAL/exposure_log.csv" <<'EOF'
import csv, sys, os
g = list(csv.DictReader(open(sys.argv[1]))) if os.path.exists(sys.argv[1]) else []
days = sorted({int(r["day"]) for r in g})
print(f"  days captured: {len(days)} ({', '.join('D'+str(d) for d in days) or 'none'})   rows: {len(g)}")
if g:
    last = max(days)
    rows = [r for r in g if int(r["day"]) == last]
    print(f"  latest D{last} at {rows[0]['timestamp']}:")
    print("    " + "  ".join(f"{r['pot_id']} {float(r.get('cell_gpr') or r['raw_gpr']):.2f}%" for r in rows))
    print("    (pot-area green pixel ratio; 0 % until the seedlings come up)")
if os.path.exists(sys.argv[2]):
    e = list(csv.DictReader(open(sys.argv[2])))
    if e:
        x = e[-1]
        print(f"  last exposure: {int(x['exposure_us'])/1000:.1f} ms, brightness {x['mean_v']} (target {x['target_v']}), light used: {x['light_used']}")
EOF
n=$(ls "$DATA"/frames/D*.png 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
echo "  raw frames saved: $n   check pictures: $(ls "$DATA"/frames/overlay/*.jpg 2>/dev/null | wc -l)  ($DATA/frames)"

echo; echo "${B}3. Light (hourly, camera used as a light meter)${N}"
if [[ $(rows "$DUAL/light_log.csv") -gt 0 ]]; then
  tail -n +2 "$DUAL/light_log.csv" | tail -n 4 | awk -F, '{printf "  %s  light index %s %s\n", $1, $6, $7}'
else echo "  no readings yet"; fi

echo; echo "${B}4. Faults and power${N}"
echo "  error log entries : $(rows "$DATA/error_log.csv")"
[[ $(rows "$DATA/error_log.csv") -gt 0 ]] && tail -n 3 "$DATA/error_log.csv" | sed 's/^/    /'
echo "  power events      : $(rows "$DATA/power_log.csv")"

echo; echo "${B}5. Deliverable files${N}"
for f in "$DUAL/growth_dataset.csv" "$DATA/sm_log.csv" "$DATA/irrigation_log.csv" "$DATA/error_log.csv" \
         "$DATA/power_log.csv" "$DUAL/light_log.csv" "$DUAL/exposure_log.csv" "$DUAL/field_log.csv" \
         "$DUAL/pot_rois.json" "$DUAL/harvest_weights.csv"; do
  if [[ $f == *.json && -f $f ]]; then printf "  %-50s %s\n" "$f" "present (9 pot circles)"
  elif [[ -f $f ]]; then printf "  %-50s %5s rows\n" "$f" "$(rows "$f")"
  else printf "  %-50s %s\n" "$f" "(not yet -- created at harvest)"; fi
done
echo; echo "Open a file as a table:  column -s, -t < FILE | less -S    (q to quit)"


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\raw_watch.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""raw_watch.py -- stream raw soil ADC for all 9 pots, once a second.

For probe tests (air / water cup / swap). Stops pi-link to own the serial port;
restart it afterwards with:  sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link

    python3 tools/raw_watch.py            # until Ctrl-C
    python3 tools/raw_watch.py --secs 60
Calibration reference (shared by all pots): dry/air ~2483, wet/water ~1120.
"""
import argparse
import time

from esp_link import EspLink, stop_daemon

ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
ap.add_argument("--secs", type=float, default=0, help="stop after this many seconds (0 = forever)")
args = ap.parse_args()

stop_daemon()
t_end = time.time() + args.secs if args.secs else None
with EspLink() as esp:
    print("time      " + "".join(f"P-0{i}  " for i in range(1, 10)), flush=True)
    try:
        while t_end is None or time.time() < t_end:
            r = esp.ask("GET_RAW", "RAW", 8)
            if r and r.startswith("RAW"):
                vals = r.split(",")[2:]
                print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S  ") + "".join(f"{v:>5} " for v in vals), flush=True)
            else:
                print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S  ") + f"no reply ({r})", flush=True)
            time.sleep(1)
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        pass
print("pi-link is STOPPED. Restart: sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\recompute_cell_gpr.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
recompute_cell_gpr.py -- GPR inside each pot's own CELL, recomputed from the
saved noon frames.

Why: from about D16 the seedlings lean toward the light and grow out past the
pot rim, so the pot-circle GPR (raw_gpr) drops to 0 although the plants are
alive. The old territory circle (terr_gpr, r = 221 px) is big enough, but
neighbouring territory circles overlap (real spacing is 25-31 cm, the circle
assumes 33 cm), so a leaf could count for two pots.

The CELL of a pot = every pixel closer to that pot's centre than to any other
pot's centre (a Voronoi cell), capped at CELL_R_PX from the centre. Cells never
overlap, and leaning leaves stay inside their own pot's cell.

The green mask is exactly the live pipeline's (capture_gpr.green_mask: same HSV
band, same full-frame 3x3 opening). Pot centres come from the ROI file that was
live on that day (they moved on D16 and D22 when the camera was re-aligned).
The live capture is NOT changed by this script.

USAGE (Pi or Mac; needs opencv + numpy)
    python3 tools/recompute_cell_gpr.py                      # all frames, D12+
    python3 tools/recompute_cell_gpr.py --frames-dir DIR --out cell_gpr.csv
    python3 tools/recompute_cell_gpr.py --overlay-day 21     # also save a picture
"""

import argparse
import csv
import glob
import json
import os
import re
import sys

import cv2
import numpy as np

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
DUAL = os.path.dirname(HERE)
sys.path.insert(0, DUAL)
import capture_gpr as cg  # noqa: E402  (same mask code as the live pipeline)

CELL_R_PX = cg.CELL_R_PX  # same cell as the live capture (run 2 writes cell_gpr itself)

# Which ROI file was live from which day on, per run (keyed by sowing date).
# Run 1 (sown 2026-10-06, archived): frames exist from D12.
ROI_SCHEDULES = {
    "2026-10-06": [
        (11, "pot_rois_before_20261021.json"),   # D11-D15: rims re-fitted Oct 16
        (16, "pot_rois_before_20261027.json"),   # D16-D21: shifted after the camera knock
        (22, "pot_rois_before_run2.json"),        # D22-D23: shifted for drift Oct 27
    ],
}
DEFAULT_SCHEDULE = [(1, "pot_rois.json")]       # any later run: add rows here if ROIs move
ROI_SCHEDULE = ROI_SCHEDULES.get(cg.sowing_date(), DEFAULT_SCHEDULE)


def rois_for_day(day):
    name = None
    for start, fname in ROI_SCHEDULE:
        if day >= start:
            name = fname
    if name is None:
        return None
    with open(os.path.join(DUAL, name)) as f:
        return json.load(f)["rois"]


def cells(shape_hw, rois):
    """{pot_id: bool mask} -- the live pipeline's cells (capture_gpr.cell_masks)."""
    return {p: m > 0 for p, m in cg.cell_masks(shape_hw, rois, CELL_R_PX).items()}


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.split("\n")[1])
    ap.add_argument("--frames-dir", default=cg.FRAME_DIR)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=os.path.join(cg.BASE, "cell_gpr.csv"))
    ap.add_argument("--overlay-day", type=int, help="save an overlay picture for this day")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    frames = {}
    for path in glob.glob(os.path.join(args.frames_dir, "D*_*.png")):
        name = os.path.basename(path)
        m = re.match(r"D(\d+)_\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}_(\d{4})\.png$", name)   # noon frames only
        if m:
            frames[int(m.group(1))] = path
    if not frames:
        sys.exit(f"no noon frames found in {args.frames_dir}")

    rows = []
    for day in sorted(frames):
        rois = rois_for_day(day)
        img = cv2.imread(frames[day])
        if rois is None or img is None:
            continue
        green = cg.green_mask(cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)) > 0
        cm = cells(img.shape[:2], rois)
        for pid in sorted(cm):
            total = int(cm[pid].sum())
            g = int((green & cm[pid]).sum())
            rows.append({"day": day, "frame": os.path.basename(frames[day]), "pot_id": pid,
                         "cell_gpr": f"{100.0 * g / total:.2f}", "cell_green_px": g,
                         "cell_total_px": total, "cell_r_px": CELL_R_PX})
        if args.overlay_day == day:
            o = img.copy()
            for pid, mask in cm.items():
                cnt, _ = cv2.findContours(mask.astype(np.uint8), cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE)
                cv2.drawContours(o, cnt, -1, (0, 255, 255), 2)
            o[green] = (255, 0, 255)
            out_png = os.path.splitext(args.out)[0] + f"_D{day}.png"
            cv2.imwrite(out_png, o)
            print(f"overlay -> {out_png}")

    with open(args.out, "w", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=list(rows[0]))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)
    print(f"{len(rows)} rows (D{min(frames)}-D{max(frames)}) -> {args.out}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\roi_view.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""roi_view.py -- fresh camera frame with the pot circles (ROIs), each pot's
counted area and the counted green pixels drawn on it, plus GPR per pot.
Writes /tmp/roi_view.jpg. Writes no data. Used by roi_view.sh on the Mac.
"""
import json
import os
import sys
from datetime import datetime

import cv2
import numpy as np

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
import capture_gpr as cg  # noqa: E402

out = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "/tmp/roi_view.jpg"
rois = json.load(open(cg.ROI_FILE if os.path.exists(cg.ROI_FILE) else "pot_rois.json"))["rois"]
f = cg.capture_frame(use_light=False)
g = cg.green_mask(cv2.cvtColor(f, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV))
o = f.copy()
o[g > 0] = (255, 0, 255)
cells = cg.cell_masks(f.shape[:2], rois)
for p, m in cells.items():
    cnt, _ = cv2.findContours(m, cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE)
    cv2.drawContours(o, cnt, -1, (255, 255, 0), 2)
for p, r in rois.items():
    cx, cy, rad = cg.roi_geometry(r)
    cv2.circle(o, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(rad), (0, 255, 255), 3)
    c = int(np.count_nonzero(cv2.bitwise_and(g, g, mask=cells[p])))
    t = int(np.count_nonzero(cells[p]))
    for col, th in (((0, 0, 0), 6), ((255, 255, 255), 2)):
        cv2.putText(o, p, (int(cx) - 45, int(cy) - int(rad) - 14), cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 1.1, col, th)
        cv2.putText(o, f"GPR {100 * c / t:.2f}%", (int(cx) - 70, int(cy) + int(rad) + 34),
                    cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.8, col, th)
band = np.full((70, o.shape[1], 3), 30, np.uint8)
cv2.putText(band, f"AgriSentry  {datetime.now():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}   yellow = pot circle (ROI)   "
            "cyan = pot area   magenta = counted green", (12, 45),
            cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.62, (255, 255, 255), 2)
cv2.imwrite(out, np.vstack([band, o]), [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 90])
print(f"saved {out}")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\valve_sequence.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""valve_sequence.py -- run each pot's valve + pump in order, P-01 .. P-09.

For checking which hose each valve feeds: take the hoses OUT of the pots (into
a bucket) first. Every step pumps real water (MANUAL_WATER, not counted as an
irrigation event). Stops pi-link; restart it afterwards:
    sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link

    python3 tools/valve_sequence.py --ms 2000 --gap 10
"""
import argparse
import time

from esp_link import EspLink, stop_daemon

ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
ap.add_argument("--ms", type=int, default=2000, help="pump time per valve (max 15000)")
ap.add_argument("--gap", type=float, default=10, help="seconds between valves")
ap.add_argument("--pots", default="1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9")
args = ap.parse_args()

stop_daemon()
with EspLink() as esp:
    print("starting in 5 s", flush=True)
    time.sleep(5)
    for pot in [int(p) for p in args.pots.split(",")]:
        print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S") + f"  VALVE P-0{pot} + pump, {args.ms} ms", flush=True)
        r = esp.ask(f"MANUAL_WATER,{pot - 1},{min(args.ms, 15000)}", ("ACK,MANUAL_WATER", "ERR"),
                    args.ms / 1000 + 4)
        print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S") + f"  -> {r}", flush=True)
        time.sleep(args.gap)
print("done. pi-link is STOPPED. Restart: sudo -n systemctl restart pi-link")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\tools\wifi_watchdog.sh ===

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# wifi_watchdog.sh -- run every 2 min by wifi-watchdog.timer.
# If the Pi is not on any Wi-Fi, rescan and try the known networks again, so it
# joins the hotspot within ~2 min of it being switched on. Cheap and harmless
# when already connected (it just exits).
state=$(nmcli -t -f STATE general 2>/dev/null)
[[ $state == connected* ]] && exit 0
LOG=/home/crk/agrisentry_data/wifi_watchdog.log
nmcli radio wifi on
nmcli device wifi rescan >/dev/null 2>&1
sleep 5
for c in Cvtan preconfigured; do
  if nmcli -w 20 connection up "$c" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "$(date '+%F %T') reconnected via $c" >> "$LOG"; exit 0
  fi
done
echo "$(date '+%F %T') no known network in range (state: ${state:-unknown})" >> "$LOG"
# keep the log small
tail -n 500 "$LOG" > "$LOG.tmp" && mv "$LOG.tmp" "$LOG"


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\2026-09-15-camera-tools-guide.md ===

# Camera Tools â€” Paths, `cam_check.py`, and `stream_preview.py`

Group B thesis rig Â· 15 September 2026

Three parts: how Linux paths work, then each tool.

---

# PART 1 â€” Paths on the Pi

Everything about "where do the images go" comes down to one distinction.

## Absolute vs relative

An **absolute path** starts with `/`. It means the same thing no matter where you are standing:

```
/home/ced/thesis/camcheck/capture.jpg
```

A **relative path** does not start with `/`. It is interpreted from your *current directory*:

```
camcheck/capture.jpg        -> <wherever you are>/camcheck/capture.jpg
```

`pwd` tells you where you are standing. `cd` moves you.

```bash
pwd                 # /home/ced
cd ~/thesis
pwd                 # /home/ced/thesis
```

**This is the single most common source of "where did my file go?"** If you run a script from a different directory than usual and it saved to a relative path, the output moved with you.

## The four shorthands

| Symbol | Means | Example |
|---|---|---|
| `~` | your home directory | `~/thesis` â†’ `/home/ced/thesis` |
| `.` | the current directory | `./capture.jpg` |
| `..` | one directory up | `../pot_rois.json` |
| `/` | the root of the filesystem | `/media/usb` |

`~` is expanded by the **shell**, not by Python. That matters:

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --out-dir ~/Pictures     # shell expands ~ -> /home/ced/Pictures  âœ“
python3 cam_check.py --out-dir '~/Pictures'   # quoted: shell does NOT expand it
```

The quoted version would normally create a literal folder named `~`. `cam_check.py` calls
`os.path.expanduser()` on whatever it receives, so it survives that mistake â€” but do not
rely on every program being that forgiving.

## Spaces in paths

A space separates arguments. A path with a space in it must be quoted:

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --out-dir "~/My Photos/day 0"     # âœ“
python3 cam_check.py --out-dir ~/My Photos/day 0       # âœ— three arguments
```

This is why Linux people use `day0` and `day_0` instead of `day 0`.

## Where things actually live on your Pi

```
/home/ced/                      your home. "~" is this.
â””â”€â”€ thesis/                     THESIS_BASE
    â”œâ”€â”€ capture_gpr.py
    â”œâ”€â”€ pi_bench.py
    â”œâ”€â”€ cam_check.py
    â”œâ”€â”€ pot_rois.json           the nine ROI circles
    â”œâ”€â”€ growth_dataset.csv      the 315 rows you are building
    â””â”€â”€ camcheck/               images from cam_check.py   <- OUT_DIR
        â”œâ”€â”€ capture.jpg
        â”œâ”€â”€ capture_mask.png
        â”œâ”€â”€ rois.jpg
        â””â”€â”€ focus_0.82.jpg
```

## Checking and moving files

```bash
ls ~/thesis/camcheck/                 # what is in there
ls -lh ~/thesis/camcheck/             # with sizes and dates
du -sh ~/thesis/camcheck/             # how big is the folder
realpath ~/thesis/camcheck            # resolve to an absolute path
df -h /                               # free space on the SD card
```

## Getting images onto your laptop

You cannot view a JPEG over plain SSH. Three ways:

**1. `scp`** â€” from your *laptop's* PowerShell, not from inside SSH:

```powershell
scp ced@groupb.local:~/thesis/camcheck/*.jpg .
scp -r ced@groupb.local:~/thesis/camcheck "C:\Users\ACER\ThesisCoWork\camcheck"
```

The `.` at the end means "into my current folder". `-r` means recursive, for a whole directory.

**2. A one-off web server** â€” quickest for a quick look:

```bash
cd ~/thesis/camcheck
python3 -m http.server 8080
```

Then open `http://groupb.local:8080/` in your browser. Ctrl-C to stop. Anyone on your
network can read that folder while it runs, so stop it when you are done.

**3. VS Code Remote-SSH** â€” connect to the Pi and images open in the editor. Most
comfortable if you are already living in VS Code.

---

# PART 2 â€” `cam_check.py`

Not a live view. It asks: **is the image good enough to measure from?**

## How it decides where to save

Three levels, each overriding the one above:

```
1. built-in default     ~/thesis/camcheck
2. environment variable CAMCHECK_OUT, or THESIS_BASE
3. command-line flag    --out-dir          <- wins
```

```bash
# 1. default
python3 cam_check.py --check
#    [cam_check] images -> /home/ced/thesis/camcheck

# 2. for this shell session only
export CAMCHECK_OUT=~/Pictures/camcheck
python3 cam_check.py --check
#    [cam_check] images -> /home/ced/Pictures/camcheck

# 3. just this one run
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir /media/usb/day0
#    [cam_check] images -> /media/usb/day0
```

`export` lasts until you close the SSH session. To make it permanent, add the line to
`~/.bashrc`. `unset CAMCHECK_OUT` cancels it.

`THESIS_BASE` moves the images **and** `pot_rois.json` together:

```bash
export THESIS_BASE=/mnt/ssd/thesis
# -> images  /mnt/ssd/thesis/camcheck
# -> rois    /mnt/ssd/thesis/pot_rois.json
```

The first line of every run prints where it is writing. **Read that line.** It is there
so you never have to guess.

## Filenames are fixed â€” this matters

Each run writes the same names and overwrites the last:

`capture.jpg` Â· `capture_mask.png` Â· `check_1.jpg` Â· `check_2.jpg` Â· `rois.jpg` Â· `focus_<lp>.jpg`

So to compare two setups, give each its own folder:

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/camcheck/h1650
# raise the camera
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/camcheck/h1350
```

Now `~/camcheck/h1650/rois.jpg` and `~/camcheck/h1350/rois.jpg` sit side by side and you
can actually see which height keeps all nine pots in frame.

Dated folders work well for daily checks:

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/camcheck/$(date +%F)
# $(date +%F) expands to 2026-09-15
```

## The five modes

### `--info` â€” start here

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --info
```

Lists cameras and sensor modes. No image is captured, nothing is saved.

Look for **`imx708`** (Camera Module 3, VFOV 41Â°) or **`imx219`** (Module V2, VFOV 48.8Â°).
Your manuscript Section III.B still names the V2 â€” different geometry, different mounting
height. This tells you which one is really on the rig.

Also check that a sensor mode matches **2304Ã—1296**. If none does, libcamera crops or
scales, and a crop means a narrower field of view than your height calculation assumed.

### `--capture` â€” one still, with numbers

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --capture
python3 cam_check.py --capture --auto     # same shot with AE/AF left on
```

Saves `capture.jpg` and `capture_mask.png`, and prints:

| Reading | Want | If it is wrong |
|---|---|---|
| mean brightness | 80â€“180 | under 60, `V>40` rejects shadowed leaf, GPR reads low |
| blown-out % | < 1 % | clipped leaves lose saturation, drop out of the band, GPR reads **low** |
| crushed black % | < 1 % | shadow detail gone |
| sharpness | compare only | meaningless as an absolute number |
| green pixels % | â€” | your actual measurement |

Running it twice â€” once normally, once with `--auto` â€” shows you what locking the camera
is actually doing.

**Open `capture_mask.png`.** Leaves white, everything else black. That one image tells you
whether the HSV band is selecting your Pechay, and it is the fastest check in the whole
toolkit.

### `--focus-sweep` â€” the one with 35-day consequences

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --focus-sweep --distance 1.22
```

`LensPosition` is measured in **dioptres = 1 Ã· distance in metres.**

| Distance to subject | LensPosition |
|---|---|
| infinity | 0.0 |
| 2.0 m | 0.50 |
| 1.22 m | 0.82 |
| 1.0 m | 1.00 |
| 0.40 m | 2.50 |

`--distance` is lens to **canopy**, not lens to floor. At 1650 mm camera height with
~430 mm of pot-plus-plant, that is 1.22 m.

The sweep steps through nine positions around your estimate, scores each by Laplacian
variance, saves every frame, and recommends one. Put a printed page of text at the canopy
plane before running it â€” focusing on bare soil gives the algorithm nothing to grip.

Then hardcode the winner in `capture_gpr.py`:

```python
"LensPosition": 0.82,
```

**Why this matters more than it looks.** If autofocus hunts between days, edge sharpness
changes and the green pixel count drifts with no change in the plant. Your model would be
fitting lens behaviour, not growth.

Two cautions the script prints for you: if the best value lands at the *edge* of the
sweep, the true optimum is outside it â€” re-run with a better `--distance`. And Laplacian
variance also rewards noise and moving leaves, so confirm by eye in `focus_*.jpg`.

### `--rois` â€” the silent-failure check

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --rois
```

Captures one frame, overlays the circles from `pot_rois.json`, prints a table, saves
`rois.jpg`. Green circle = inside the frame. **Red = clipped.**

A clipped ROI does not raise an error. It measures a smaller area, and that pot's GPR is
wrong for all 35 days while looking perfectly plausible in the CSV. This is the check that
catches it before the data exists.

Point it at a different ROI file with `--roi-file`:

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --rois --roi-file ~/thesis/rois_v2.json
```

### `--check` â€” everything

```bash
python3 cam_check.py --check
```

Runs `--info`, a locked capture, **a repeatability test**, then `--rois`.

The repeatability test is the part you cannot get anywhere else: two frames five seconds
apart with nothing moving. With controls locked they should be near-identical.

| Reading | Want |
|---|---|
| green delta | < 0.10 pp |
| brightness delta | < 2.0 |

Fail either and something is still auto-adjusting. **An unstable camera makes GPR
unusable** â€” you would be measuring the camera, not the plant.

Run this before D=1. Then open every file it wrote.

---

# PART 3 â€” `stream_preview.py`

Different job. `cam_check.py` signs off on a camera that is already pointed correctly.
`stream_preview.py` is how you point it â€” a live browser view while your hands are on the
mount.

## Start it

```bash
cd ~/thesis
python3 stream_preview.py
```

It prints its own URL:

```
[preview] 1024x576  ->  http://192.168.1.3:8000/
[preview] Ctrl-C to stop
```

Open that on your laptop. Ctrl-C on the Pi to stop.

## Its flags

```bash
python3 stream_preview.py --port 8080
python3 stream_preview.py --width 1280
python3 stream_preview.py --roi-file ~/thesis/rois_v2.json
python3 stream_preview.py --lock-exposure
```

**It saves nothing** â€” that is why it has no `--out-dir`. It is a live stream only. To
keep a frame, use `cam_check.py --capture`.

Keep `--width` at a **16:9** value (1024, 1280, 1600). The still capture is 2304Ã—1296,
which is 16:9, and the ROI overlay scales by that assumption. A 4:3 width misaligns every
circle.

## The four modes

Buttons on the page, or `?mode=` in the URL.

| Mode | Shows | Use it to |
|---|---|---|
| `normal` | thirds grid + centre crosshair | level the frame, centre the array |
| `roi` | the nine circles from `pot_rois.json` | confirm every pot is inside â€” red = falling off |
| `mask` | only what the HSV band selects | point at real leaves; they must light up |
| `gpr` | circles + live per-pot % | watch the number move as you adjust |

## The order to use them in

1. **`normal`** â€” get the array square in the frame using the grid.
2. **`roi`** â€” every circle green. One red circle means raise the camera or re-run `make_rois.py`.
3. **`mask`** â€” hold a real Pechay leaf in view. It must turn white. If leaves stay black, your HSV band is wrong.
4. **`gpr`** â€” final sanity check that the numbers are plausible before you walk away.

## On `mask` mode

This is the HSV calibration task. The band is `H 35â€“85` in **OpenCV hue units (0â€“179)**,
not degrees.

Do **not** "convert" 35â€“85 to 17â€“42 by halving. That was tested and returns 0.00 % GPR on a
healthy green canopy, because it selects orange-yellow instead of green. Keep the code as
it is and fix the manuscript text, not the other way round.

## On `--lock-exposure`

Without it the preview auto-exposes and looks nicer. With it, you see what
`capture_gpr.py` will actually see.

**Aim with `--lock-exposure` on.** A preview that has quietly brightened the scene is not
telling you the truth about your measurement.

Note the flag currently applies `LensPosition: 2.5`, which is 40 cm â€” wrong for this rig.
Once `--focus-sweep` gives you the real value, change it in `stream_preview.py` too so both
tools agree.

## Startup line worth reading

```
[preview] sensor output: (2304, 1296)  (want 2304x1296)
```

If those disagree, the preview's field of view is not the same as the still capture, and
aiming with it is not valid. Tell me if that happens.

---

# Which tool, when

| Situation | Tool |
|---|---|
| mounting or moving the camera | `stream_preview.py`, mode `roi` |
| checking the HSV band on real leaves | `stream_preview.py`, mode `mask` |
| choosing a mounting height | `cam_check.py --rois --out-dir` per height |
| locking focus | `cam_check.py --focus-sweep` |
| signing off before D=1 | `cam_check.py --check` |
| daily spot-check during the run | `cam_check.py --capture --out-dir ~/camcheck/$(date +%F)` |

**Never run both at once.** The camera is a single-access device â€” the second one to start
will fail with a "device busy" error.

---

# Cheat sheet

```bash
# where am I, what is here
pwd ; ls -lh ~/thesis/camcheck/

# change output for one run
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/camcheck/test1

# change it for the session
export CAMCHECK_OUT=~/Pictures/camcheck

# move rois AND images together
export THESIS_BASE=/mnt/ssd/thesis

# dated folder
python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/camcheck/$(date +%F)

# pull to laptop (run in PowerShell, not in SSH)
scp -r ced@groupb.local:~/thesis/camcheck "C:\Users\ACER\ThesisCoWork\camcheck"

# quick browser look instead
cd ~/thesis/camcheck && python3 -m http.server 8080

# aim the rig
python3 stream_preview.py --lock-exposure
```


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\analyze_yield.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
analyze_yield.py -- fit the yield model and validate it.  Deliverables 5 and 6.

Thesis: Computer Vision-Based Growth Monitoring and Yield Prediction for
        Pechay Under Differential Soil Moisture Treatments in a Greenhouse

Does four things:
  1. Fills the smooth_gpr column with a 3-day centred moving average per plant.
     capture_gpr.py deliberately leaves this blank because a centred window
     needs days on both sides, so it can only be computed once the run is over.
  2. Fits the six-term model from Section III.F by ordinary least squares.
  3. Validates it with leave-one-PLANT-out cross-validation.
  4. Fits the original eight-term model the same way, so the manuscript's
     overfitting claim is TESTED rather than asserted.

THE MODEL
    Y = b0 + b1*D + b2*GPR + b3*SM + b4*GPR^2 + b5*(GPR x SM) + e

    Y is one number per plant -- its fresh weight at harvest. D, GPR and SM
    vary daily. So all 35 rows for a plant share the same Y, and the model
    reads as: "from a snapshot on day D showing this canopy and this moisture,
    what will this plant finally weigh?"

WHY LEAVE-ONE-PLANT-OUT AND NOT A RANDOM SPLIT
    The 35 rows within one plant are strongly autocorrelated -- day 20 looks
    much like day 19. A random row-wise split would put day 19 in training and
    day 20 in test, so the model would be scored on rows nearly identical to
    ones it had already seen. That inflates the reported skill without the
    model generalising at all. Holding out a whole plant is the only split
    that measures what the study actually claims: prediction for a plant the
    model has never seen.

TWO ERROR FIGURES, BOTH REPORTED
    The manuscript defines MAE over "the total number of harvested plant
    validation specimens", which reads as one prediction per plant, but the
    dataset has 35 rows per plant. Both are computed here:
      per-plant  -- average the held-out plant's 35 daily predictions into one
                    predicted weight, compare with the measured weight (n = 9)
      per-row    -- every row scored individually (n = 315)
    Report the per-plant figure as the headline and state which is which.

    Only numpy is required. No scikit-learn, so this runs on the Pi as-is.

USAGE
    python3 analyze_yield.py
    python3 analyze_yield.py --growth growth_dataset.csv --harvest harvest_weights.csv
    python3 analyze_yield.py --self-test        synthetic data, no files needed
"""

import argparse
import csv
import json
import os
import sys

import numpy as np

BASE = os.path.expanduser("~/thesis")
GROWTH_CSV = os.path.join(BASE, "growth_dataset.csv")
HARVEST_CSV = os.path.join(BASE, "harvest_weights.csv")
OUT_JSON = os.path.join(BASE, "model_results.json")
OUT_CSV = os.path.join(BASE, "model_predictions.csv")

SMOOTH_WINDOW = 3          # 3-day centred moving average

# Six-term specification, Section III.F.
TERMS_6 = ["1", "D", "GPR", "SM", "GPR^2", "GPR*SM"]
# Original eight-term specification, kept for the overfitting comparison.
TERMS_8 = ["1", "D", "GPR", "SM", "D^2", "GPR^2", "D*GPR", "GPR*SM"]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# data
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def load_growth(path, col="cell_gpr"):
    """GPR from `col`; rows without it (run-1 files) fall back to raw_gpr."""
    rows = []
    with open(path, newline="") as f:
        for r in csv.DictReader(f):
            try:
                gpr = float(r[col] if r.get(col) not in (None, "") else r["raw_gpr"])
                sm = float(r["sm_pct"])       # "NaN" raises, row dropped
                d = int(r["day"])
            except (KeyError, ValueError):
                continue
            rows.append({"pot_id": r["pot_id"], "day": d, "gpr": gpr, "sm": sm,
                         "flag": int(r.get("flag", 0) or 0)})
    return rows


def load_harvest(path):
    out = {}
    with open(path, newline="") as f:
        for r in csv.DictReader(f):
            try:
                out[r["pot_id"]] = float(r["fresh_weight_g"])
            except (KeyError, ValueError):
                continue
    return out


def smooth_per_plant(rows, window=SMOOTH_WINDOW):
    """3-day centred moving average of GPR, computed within each plant.

    At day 1 and day 35 the window is truncated rather than dropped, so no
    observation is lost at the ends of the series."""
    half = window // 2
    by_pot = {}
    for r in rows:
        by_pot.setdefault(r["pot_id"], []).append(r)
    for pot, rs in by_pot.items():
        rs.sort(key=lambda x: x["day"])
        vals = [x["gpr"] for x in rs]
        for i, r in enumerate(rs):
            lo, hi = max(0, i - half), min(len(vals), i + half + 1)
            r["gpr_s"] = float(np.mean(vals[lo:hi]))
    return rows


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# model
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def design(rows, terms, use_smoothed=True):
    D = np.array([r["day"] for r in rows], float)
    G = np.array([r["gpr_s"] if use_smoothed else r["gpr"] for r in rows], float)
    S = np.array([r["sm"] for r in rows], float)
    col = {"1": np.ones_like(D), "D": D, "GPR": G, "SM": S,
           "D^2": D ** 2, "GPR^2": G ** 2, "D*GPR": D * G, "GPR*SM": G * S}
    return np.column_stack([col[t] for t in terms])


def fit_ols(X, y):
    """Least squares via lstsq, which handles rank deficiency gracefully
    instead of raising on a singular normal-equations matrix."""
    beta, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(X, y, rcond=None)
    return beta


def loocv_by_plant(rows, weights, terms, use_smoothed=True):
    """Hold out one whole plant at a time. Returns per-row and per-plant error."""
    pots = sorted({r["pot_id"] for r in rows})
    row_pred, row_true, per_plant = [], [], []

    for held in pots:
        tr = [r for r in rows if r["pot_id"] != held]
        te = [r for r in rows if r["pot_id"] == held]
        if not te:
            continue

        Xtr = design(tr, terms, use_smoothed)
        ytr = np.array([weights[r["pot_id"]] for r in tr], float)
        beta = fit_ols(Xtr, ytr)

        Xte = design(te, terms, use_smoothed)
        pred = Xte @ beta
        true = np.array([weights[r["pot_id"]] for r in te], float)

        row_pred.extend(pred.tolist())
        row_true.extend(true.tolist())
        per_plant.append({"pot_id": held,
                          "actual_g": float(weights[held]),
                          "predicted_g": float(np.mean(pred)),
                          "n_rows": len(te)})

    row_pred = np.array(row_pred)
    row_true = np.array(row_true)
    pa = np.array([p["actual_g"] for p in per_plant])
    pp = np.array([p["predicted_g"] for p in per_plant])

    return {
        "per_row": metrics(row_true, row_pred),
        "per_plant": metrics(pa, pp),
        "plants": per_plant,
    }


def metrics(actual, pred):
    """R-squared and MAE.

    R^2 is computed against the mean of the ACTUAL values, so out-of-sample it
    can legitimately go negative -- that means the model does worse than simply
    predicting the mean weight, which is a real and reportable result, not a
    bug."""
    ss_res = float(np.sum((actual - pred) ** 2))
    ss_tot = float(np.sum((actual - np.mean(actual)) ** 2))
    r2 = 1.0 - ss_res / ss_tot if ss_tot > 0 else float("nan")
    return {"r2": r2,
            "mae": float(np.mean(np.abs(actual - pred))),
            "rmse": float(np.sqrt(np.mean((actual - pred) ** 2))),
            "n": int(len(actual))}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def report(name, terms, rows, weights, use_smoothed=True):
    X = design(rows, terms, use_smoothed)
    y = np.array([weights[r["pot_id"]] for r in rows], float)
    beta = fit_ols(X, y)
    insample = metrics(y, X @ beta)
    cv = loocv_by_plant(rows, weights, terms, use_smoothed)

    print(f"\n=== {name} ({len(terms)} terms) ===")
    print("  coefficients (fitted on all data):")
    for t, b in zip(terms, beta):
        label = "b0 (intercept)" if t == "1" else t
        print(f"    {label:<16} {b:+12.4f}")
    print(f"  in-sample     R2 = {insample['r2']:7.4f}   "
          f"MAE = {insample['mae']:7.2f} g   (n={insample['n']})")
    print(f"  LOOCV per-row R2 = {cv['per_row']['r2']:7.4f}   "
          f"MAE = {cv['per_row']['mae']:7.2f} g   (n={cv['per_row']['n']})")
    print(f"  LOOCV per-pl. R2 = {cv['per_plant']['r2']:7.4f}   "
          f"MAE = {cv['per_plant']['mae']:7.2f} g   (n={cv['per_plant']['n']})")

    return {"terms": terms,
            "coefficients": {t: float(b) for t, b in zip(terms, beta)},
            "in_sample": insample, "loocv": cv}


def synthetic(n_plants=9, n_days=35, seed=1):
    """Nine plants on a plausible sigmoid, for testing the machinery without
    waiting 35 days. Not a substitute for real data."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    bands = {0: (35, 260), 1: (55, 330), 2: (75, 300)}   # SM mean, final grams
    rows, weights = [], {}
    for p in range(n_plants):
        pid = f"P-0{p+1}"
        sm_mu, final = bands[p // 3]
        final += rng.normal(0, 18)
        weights[pid] = float(final)
        for d in range(1, n_days + 1):
            gpr = 85 / (1 + np.exp(-0.28 * (d - 18))) + rng.normal(0, 1.2)
            rows.append({"pot_id": pid, "day": d,
                         "gpr": float(max(0.0, gpr)),
                         "sm": float(sm_mu + rng.normal(0, 2.5)),
                         "flag": 0})
    return rows, weights


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Fit and validate the yield model")
    ap.add_argument("--growth", default=GROWTH_CSV)
    ap.add_argument("--harvest", default=HARVEST_CSV)
    ap.add_argument("--raw-gpr", action="store_true",
                    help="use raw instead of smoothed GPR")
    ap.add_argument("--gpr-column", default="cell_gpr",
                    help="growth_dataset.csv column used as GPR (default cell_gpr = "
                         "pot-area GPR; raw_gpr = the pot circle)")
    ap.add_argument("--self-test", action="store_true",
                    help="run on synthetic data, write nothing")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if args.self_test:
        rows, weights = synthetic()
        print(f"[self-test] {len(rows)} synthetic rows, "
              f"{len(weights)} plants -- NOT real data")
    else:
        for path in (args.growth, args.harvest):
            if not os.path.exists(path):
                sys.exit(f"missing {path}. Run --self-test to check the "
                         f"analysis without real data.")
        rows = load_growth(args.growth, args.gpr_column)
        print(f"[analyze] GPR column: {args.gpr_column}")
        weights = load_harvest(args.harvest)
        missing = {r["pot_id"] for r in rows} - set(weights)
        if missing:
            sys.exit(f"no harvest weight for {sorted(missing)} -- "
                     f"run record_harvest.py first")
        print(f"[analyze] {len(rows)} usable rows, {len(weights)} plants")
        flagged = sum(r["flag"] for r in rows)
        if flagged:
            print(f"[analyze] {flagged} rows carry an anomaly flag; they are "
                  f"INCLUDED. Re-run excluding them to see the difference.")

    rows = smooth_per_plant(rows)
    use_smoothed = not args.raw_gpr
    print(f"[analyze] GPR: {'3-day centred average' if use_smoothed else 'raw'}")

    six = report("Six-term specification (manuscript)", TERMS_6,
                 rows, weights, use_smoothed)
    eight = report("Eight-term specification (original)", TERMS_8,
                   rows, weights, use_smoothed)

    print("\n=== overfitting check ===")
    d_in = eight["in_sample"]["r2"] - six["in_sample"]["r2"]
    d_cv = eight["loocv"]["per_plant"]["r2"] - six["loocv"]["per_plant"]["r2"]
    print(f"  eight-term minus six-term, in-sample R2 : {d_in:+.4f}")
    print(f"  eight-term minus six-term, LOOCV R2     : {d_cv:+.4f}")
    EPS = 0.005      # below this the difference is numerical noise, not signal
    if abs(d_in) < EPS and abs(d_cv) < EPS:
        print("  -> the two specifications are indistinguishable on this data.")
        print("     Parsimony still favours six terms, but do NOT claim the")
        print("     eight-term model overfits: this run does not show it.")
    elif d_in > EPS and d_cv < -EPS:
        print("  -> eight terms fit the training data better but generalise")
        print("     worse. That is textbook overfitting, and it supports the")
        print("     manuscript's reason for dropping D^2 and D*GPR.")
    elif d_cv > 0:
        print("  -> eight terms generalise BETTER here. The manuscript's")
        print("     overfitting claim is not supported by this data and the")
        print("     text needs revising to match what was actually found.")
    else:
        print("  -> difference is not clear-cut; report both and say so.")

    if args.self_test:
        print("\n[self-test] machinery works. Nothing written.")
        return

    with open(OUT_JSON, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"six_term": six, "eight_term": eight}, f, indent=2)
    with open(OUT_CSV, "w", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f)
        w.writerow(["pot_id", "actual_g", "predicted_g", "abs_error_g"])
        for p in six["loocv"]["plants"]:
            w.writerow([p["pot_id"], f"{p['actual_g']:.1f}",
                        f"{p['predicted_g']:.1f}",
                        f"{abs(p['actual_g'] - p['predicted_g']):.1f}"])
    print(f"\nwrote {OUT_JSON}")
    print(f"wrote {OUT_CSV}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\calibrate_clean.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json, cv2, numpy as np, time
from picamera2 import Picamera2
from libcamera import controls

# 1. Grab a clean, unannotated frame with capture_gpr controls
cam = Picamera2()
cfg = cam.create_still_configuration(main={"size": (1296, 1296)})
cam.configure(cfg)
cam.start()
cam.set_controls({
    "AfMode": controls.AfModeEnum.Manual,
    "LensPosition": 0.42,
    "AeEnable": False,
    "ExposureTime": 24000,
    "AnalogueGain": 1.0,
    "AwbEnable": False,
    "ColourGains": (1.8, 1.6),
})
time.sleep(2)
raw_bgr = cv2.cvtColor(cam.capture_array(), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
cam.stop()
cam.close()
cv2.imwrite('/home/crk/clean_test_frame.jpg', raw_bgr)
print("Saved clean frame to /home/crk/clean_test_frame.jpg")

with open('/home/crk/THESIS/Dual_Archi/pot_rois.json') as f:
    cfg = json.load(f)
rois = cfg.get("rois", cfg)

hsv = cv2.cvtColor(raw_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)

print("\n--- SWEEPING HSV ON REAL PIXELS ---")
print("Target: Cotyledon pots ~0.8% - 2.5%, Empty/bare soil pots <= 0.3%")
print(f"{'HUE':<12} {'SAT':<6} {'VAL':<6} | " + " ".join([f"{k:>5}" for k in sorted(rois.keys())]))
print("-" * 75)

for h_lo, h_hi in [(35, 85), (38, 85), (40, 85)]:
    for s_min in [60, 70, 80, 90, 100]:
        for v_min in [45, 55, 65]:
            m = cv2.inRange(hsv, np.array([h_lo, s_min, v_min]), np.array([h_hi, 255, 255]))
            kernel = np.ones((3, 3), np.uint8)
            m_open = cv2.morphologyEx(m, cv2.MORPH_OPEN, kernel)
            
            vals = []
            for pid in sorted(rois.keys()):
                r = rois[pid]
                cx, cy = int(r["x"] + r["w"]/2), int(r["y"] + r["h"]/2)
                rad = int(min(r["w"], r["h"])/2)
                pmask = np.zeros(hsv.shape[:2], np.uint8)
                cv2.circle(pmask, (cx, cy), rad, 255, -1)
                tot = np.count_nonzero(pmask)
                grn = np.count_nonzero(cv2.bitwise_and(m_open, m_open, mask=pmask))
                vals.append(grn / tot * 100)
            
            # Print row
            scores = " ".join([f"{v:5.2f}%" for v in vals])
            print(f"H({h_lo}-{h_hi})   S>{s_min:<3} V>{v_min:<3} | {scores}")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\calibrate_hsv.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
import json, cv2, numpy as np

img = cv2.imread('/home/crk/roi_alignment_debug.png')
if img is None:
    raise SystemExit("Missing /home/crk/roi_alignment_debug.png")

with open('/home/crk/THESIS/Dual_Archi/pot_rois.json') as f:
    cfg = json.load(f)
rois = cfg.get("rois", cfg)

hsv = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)

print("--- HSV CALIBRATION ANALYSIS ---")
for pid in ["P-07", "P-04", "P-05"]:
    r = rois[pid]
    cx, cy = int(r["x"] + r["w"]/2), int(r["y"] + r["h"]/2)
    rad = int(min(r["w"], r["h"])/2)
    
    mask = np.zeros(hsv.shape[:2], np.uint8)
    cv2.circle(mask, (cx, cy), rad, 255, -1)
    
    pts = hsv[mask == 255]
    h, s, v = pts[:,0], pts[:,1], pts[:,2]
    
    print(f"\n[{pid}] Soil & Canopy Pixels ({len(pts)} total):")
    print(f"  Hue:        min={h.min():3d}, p25={int(np.percentile(h,25)):3d}, median={int(np.median(h)):3d}, max={h.max():3d}")
    print(f"  Saturation: min={s.min():3d}, p25={int(np.percentile(s,25)):3d}, median={int(np.median(s)):3d}, max={s.max():3d}")
    print(f"  Value:      min={v.min():3d}, p25={int(np.percentile(v,25)):3d}, median={int(np.median(v)):3d}, max={v.max():3d}")

# Test candidate thresholds across all 9 pots
for s_min, v_min in [(70, 50), (80, 55), (90, 60), (100, 65)]:
    m = cv2.inRange(hsv, np.array([35, s_min, v_min]), np.array([85, 255, 255]))
    kernel = np.ones((3, 3), np.uint8)
    m_open = cv2.morphologyEx(m, cv2.MORPH_OPEN, kernel)
    
    pot_pcts = []
    for pid, r in rois.items():
        cx, cy = int(r["x"] + r["w"]/2), int(r["y"] + r["h"]/2)
        rad = int(min(r["w"], r["h"])/2)
        pmask = np.zeros(hsv.shape[:2], np.uint8)
        cv2.circle(pmask, (cx, cy), rad, 255, -1)
        tot = np.count_nonzero(pmask)
        grn = np.count_nonzero(cv2.bitwise_and(m_open, m_open, mask=pmask))
        pot_pcts.append(round(grn / tot * 100, 2))
    print(f"\nTested H(35-85) S>{s_min} V>{v_min} -> GPR across pots: {pot_pcts}")


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\camera_setup.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
camera_setup.py -- measure the three camera controls that capture_gpr.py
needs, and verify the rig framing.

Thesis: Computer Vision-Based Growth Monitoring and Yield Prediction for
        Pechay Under Differential Soil Moisture Treatments in a Greenhouse

capture_gpr.py locks LensPosition, ExposureTime and ColourGains so that
acquisition conditions stay identical across all 35 days. The values in the
script are placeholders. This tool measures the real ones on the rig.

WHY THEY MUST BE LOCKED
    The methodology treats lighting as a strict control variable. Autofocus,
    auto-exposure and auto-white-balance all fight that. If the camera
    refocuses slightly overnight, leaf edges sharpen or soften and the green
    pixel count moves without the plant changing. If auto-exposure compensates
    for a duller morning, the HSV values shift and GPR moves for the same
    reason. Every such change becomes noise in the growth curve that looks
    exactly like biology.

COMMANDS
    python3 camera_setup.py height --array 690
        Compute the required mounting height for your measured array size.

    python3 camera_setup.py frame --height 1030
        Capture one frame and report whether the whole array is visible,
        with a grid overlay so you can judge centring and squareness.

    python3 camera_setup.py focus --height 1030
        Sweep LensPosition, score each by image sharpness, report the best.

    python3 camera_setup.py exposure
        Sweep ExposureTime, report which avoids clipping.

    python3 camera_setup.py whitebalance
        Measure ColourGains once the exposure is fixed.

    python3 camera_setup.py verify --lens 0.97 --exposure 8000 --gains 1.8,1.6
        Capture with the final locked settings for a last visual check.
"""

import argparse
import math
import os
import sys
import time

import cv2
import numpy as np

try:
    from picamera2 import Picamera2
    from libcamera import controls
except ImportError:
    print("picamera2 not found. Run on the Raspberry Pi, inside a venv created")
    print("with:  python3 -m venv --system-site-packages ~/thesis-env")
    sys.exit(1)

# Camera Module 3 standard lens, full-field
VFOV_DEG = 41.0
HFOV_DEG = 66.0
RES = (2304, 1296)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def open_cam(lens=None, exposure=None, gains=None):
    cam = Picamera2()
    cam.configure(cam.create_still_configuration(main={"size": RES}))
    cam.start()
    ctl = {}
    if lens is not None:
        ctl["AfMode"] = controls.AfModeEnum.Manual
        ctl["LensPosition"] = float(lens)
    if exposure is not None:
        ctl["AeEnable"] = False
        ctl["ExposureTime"] = int(exposure)
        ctl["AnalogueGain"] = 1.0
    if gains is not None:
        ctl["AwbEnable"] = False
        ctl["ColourGains"] = tuple(gains)
    if ctl:
        cam.set_controls(ctl)
    time.sleep(2.0)          # let the locked settings settle
    return cam


def grab(cam):
    return cv2.cvtColor(cam.capture_array(), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)


def sharpness(img):
    """Variance of the Laplacian. Higher means more high-frequency detail,
    which for a fixed scene means better focus."""
    g = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY)
    return float(cv2.Laplacian(g, cv2.CV_64F).var())


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_height(args):
    a = args.array
    vmin = (a / 2) / math.tan(math.radians(VFOV_DEG) / 2)
    print(f"\nArray to be framed : {a} x {a} mm")
    print(f"Camera v3 FOV      : {VFOV_DEG} deg vertical, {HFOV_DEG} deg horizontal")
    print("\nThe VERTICAL field of view is the narrower of the two, so it is")
    print("what limits you. A height that satisfies it will always satisfy the")
    print("horizontal direction as well.\n")
    print(f"Absolute minimum height : {vmin:.0f} mm  (array exactly fills the frame)")
    for pct, label in ((1.08, "tight"), (1.12, "recommended"), (1.20, "generous")):
        h = vmin * pct
        cov_v = 2 * h * math.tan(math.radians(VFOV_DEG) / 2)
        cov_h = 2 * h * math.tan(math.radians(HFOV_DEG) / 2)
        margin = (cov_v - a) / 2
        ppmm = RES[1] / cov_v
        print(f"  {label:<12} {h:6.0f} mm -> covers {cov_h:.0f} x {cov_v:.0f} mm, "
              f"{margin:.0f} mm margin each edge, {ppmm:.2f} px/mm")
    rec = vmin * 1.12
    print(f"\nMeasure from the POT RIM, not the floor or the bench.")
    print(f"Suggested starting LensPosition = 1 / {rec/1000:.3f} m = "
          f"{1000/rec:.2f} dioptres")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_frame(args):
    cam = open_cam(lens=(1000.0 / args.height) if args.height else None)
    img = grab(cam)
    cam.stop(); cam.close()

    out = img.copy()
    h, w = out.shape[:2]
    # thirds grid, to judge centring and squareness by eye
    for i in (1, 2):
        cv2.line(out, (w * i // 3, 0), (w * i // 3, h), (0, 255, 255), 2)
        cv2.line(out, (0, h * i // 3), (w, h * i // 3), (0, 255, 255), 2)
    cv2.line(out, (w // 2, 0), (w // 2, h), (0, 0, 255), 1)
    cv2.line(out, (0, h // 2), (w, h // 2), (0, 0, 255), 1)

    cv2.imwrite("frame_check.jpg", img)
    cv2.imwrite("frame_grid.jpg", out)
    print("\nwrote frame_check.jpg  (clean capture)")
    print("wrote frame_grid.jpg   (with thirds grid and centre cross)")
    print("\nCopy both to your laptop and check:")
    print("  1. all nine pots fully visible, none clipped at any edge")
    print("  2. a visible gap between the outermost pots and the frame edge")
    print("  3. the centre pot sits on the red cross")
    print("  4. each row of pots runs parallel to a yellow line -- if rows")
    print("     appear to converge, the camera is tilted and not truly nadir")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_focus(args):
    start = 1000.0 / args.height if args.height else 1.0
    lo = max(0.0, start - args.span)
    hi = start + args.span
    steps = np.linspace(lo, hi, args.steps)

    print(f"\nSweeping LensPosition {lo:.2f} to {hi:.2f} in {args.steps} steps.")
    print("LensPosition is in DIOPTRES = 1 / (distance in metres).")
    print(f"At {args.height} mm that is nominally {start:.2f}.\n")
    print("Put a pot with real foliage under the camera before running this.")
    print("Focusing on bare soil gives a misleading peak.\n")

    cam = open_cam(exposure=args.exposure)
    results = []
    for lp in steps:
        cam.set_controls({"AfMode": controls.AfModeEnum.Manual,
                          "LensPosition": float(lp)})
        time.sleep(1.2)
        img = grab(cam)
        s = sharpness(img)
        results.append((float(lp), s))
        bar = "#" * int(s / max(1, max(r[1] for r in results)) * 40)
        print(f"  LensPosition {lp:5.2f} -> sharpness {s:9.1f}  {bar}")
        cv2.imwrite(f"focus_{lp:.2f}.jpg", img)
    cam.stop(); cam.close()

    best = max(results, key=lambda r: r[1])
    print(f"\nSHARPEST: LensPosition = {best[0]:.2f}  (score {best[1]:.1f})")
    print(f"  implied focus distance {1.0/best[0]*1000:.0f} mm")
    print("\nLook at the saved focus_*.jpg files and confirm the peak looks")
    print("sharp to your eye too. The metric can be fooled by a frame where")
    print("something moved.")
    print(f"\nThen set in capture_gpr.py:   \"LensPosition\": {best[0]:.2f},")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_exposure(args):
    times = [1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, 16000, 32000, 64000]
    print("\nSweeping ExposureTime under your CURRENT lighting.")
    print("Run this with the LED array on and at the same time of day you")
    print("intend to capture.\n")
    cam = open_cam(lens=args.lens)
    best = None
    for et in times:
        cam.set_controls({"AeEnable": False, "ExposureTime": et,
                          "AnalogueGain": 1.0})
        time.sleep(1.5)
        img = grab(cam)
        g = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY)
        mean = g.mean()
        clipped = (g >= 254).mean() * 100
        dark = (g <= 1).mean() * 100
        flag = ""
        if clipped > 0.5:
            flag = "  <- CLIPPING, detail lost in highlights"
        elif mean < 60:
            flag = "  <- too dark"
        elif 90 <= mean <= 170 and clipped <= 0.5:
            flag = "  <- good"
            if best is None:
                best = et
        print(f"  {et:6} us -> mean {mean:6.1f}, clipped {clipped:5.2f}%, "
              f"black {dark:5.2f}%{flag}")
        cv2.imwrite(f"exposure_{et}.jpg", img)
    cam.stop(); cam.close()
    if best:
        print(f"\nSuggested: ExposureTime = {best}")
    else:
        print("\nNo setting landed in the good band. Adjust the lighting level")
        print("rather than pushing exposure to an extreme.")
    print("\nThen set in capture_gpr.py:   \"ExposureTime\": <value>,")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_whitebalance(args):
    print("\nMeasuring ColourGains with auto white balance ON, so the values")
    print("it settles on can then be locked in.\n")
    print("Place a sheet of white paper covering most of the array first.\n")
    cam = Picamera2()
    cam.configure(cam.create_still_configuration(main={"size": RES}))
    cam.start()
    cam.set_controls({"AeEnable": False, "ExposureTime": args.exposure,
                      "AnalogueGain": 1.0, "AwbEnable": True})
    time.sleep(4.0)
    md = cam.capture_metadata()
    img = grab(cam)
    cam.stop(); cam.close()
    cv2.imwrite("whitebalance_check.jpg", img)
    g = md.get("ColourGains")
    if g:
        print(f"Auto white balance settled on ColourGains = "
              f"({g[0]:.2f}, {g[1]:.2f})")
        print(f"\nThen set in capture_gpr.py:   "
              f"\"ColourGains\": ({g[0]:.2f}, {g[1]:.2f}),")
    else:
        print("Camera did not report ColourGains. Keep the defaults and note it.")
    print("\nRemove the paper before any further captures.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def cmd_verify(args):
    gains = tuple(float(x) for x in args.gains.split(","))
    cam = open_cam(lens=args.lens, exposure=args.exposure, gains=gains)
    shots = []
    for i in range(3):
        img = grab(cam)
        cv2.imwrite(f"verify_{i}.jpg", img)
        shots.append(img)
        print(f"  capture {i+1}: sharpness {sharpness(img):.1f}, "
              f"mean brightness {cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY).mean():.1f}")
        time.sleep(2)
    cam.stop(); cam.close()

    d = np.abs(shots[0].astype(int) - shots[2].astype(int)).mean()
    print(f"\nmean pixel difference between first and third capture: {d:.2f}")
    if d < 2.0:
        print("  settings are stable -- repeat captures are consistent")
    else:
        print("  !! captures differ noticeably. Something is still automatic,")
        print("     or the light is changing. Re-check that AeEnable, AwbEnable")
        print("     are False and AfMode is Manual.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Camera rig setup and calibration")
    sp = ap.add_subparsers(dest="cmd", required=True)

    p = sp.add_parser("height"); p.add_argument("--array", type=float, required=True)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_height)

    p = sp.add_parser("frame"); p.add_argument("--height", type=float)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_frame)

    p = sp.add_parser("focus")
    p.add_argument("--height", type=float, required=True)
    p.add_argument("--span", type=float, default=0.6)
    p.add_argument("--steps", type=int, default=13)
    p.add_argument("--exposure", type=int, default=8000)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_focus)

    p = sp.add_parser("exposure"); p.add_argument("--lens", type=float, required=True)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_exposure)

    p = sp.add_parser("whitebalance")
    p.add_argument("--exposure", type=int, required=True)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_whitebalance)

    p = sp.add_parser("verify")
    p.add_argument("--lens", type=float, required=True)
    p.add_argument("--exposure", type=int, required=True)
    p.add_argument("--gains", required=True)
    p.set_defaults(func=cmd_verify)

    args = ap.parse_args()
    args.func(args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\cam_check.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
cam_check.py -- camera acceptance test for the GPR pipeline. Group B thesis rig.

This is NOT a live view. cam_stream.py already shows you a picture. This asks a
different question: is the picture GOOD ENOUGH TO MEASURE FROM?

A stream that looks fine to your eye can still be:
  * hunting focus between days      -> edge sharpness drifts -> GPR drifts
  * auto-exposing                   -> HSV values move when the weather does
  * cropped to a smaller sensor mode-> narrower FOV than capture_gpr.py assumes
  * framed so a pot ROI falls off   -> that pot silently reads 0 for 35 days
None of those show up as "the camera is broken". All of them corrupt the data.

RUN IN THIS ORDER
  1. python3 cam_check.py --info          is the camera there, what modes exist
  2. python3 cam_check.py --capture       one still + quality numbers
  3. python3 cam_check.py --focus-sweep   find the LensPosition to lock
  4. python3 cam_check.py --rois          are all nine pots inside the frame
  5. python3 cam_check.py --check         full pass/fail, run before D=1

WHERE THE IMAGES GO
  Default: a camcheck/ folder NEXT TO THIS SCRIPT. Run it from
  ~/THESIS/Dual_Archi and they land in ~/THESIS/Dual_Archi/camcheck.
  Every run prints the destination as its first line. Change it with:

      python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir ~/Pictures/day0
      python3 cam_check.py --check --out-dir /media/usb/camcheck
      export CAMCHECK_OUT=~/Pictures/camcheck        # for this shell session

  Every run OVERWRITES the same filenames (capture.jpg, check_1.jpg, ...), so
  use a per-session --out-dir if you want to compare two mounting heights or
  two days side by side. Nothing here is ever deleted -- it just gets replaced.

Look at the images. The numbers narrow down where to look; your eyes make the
call.
"""

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
import time

# WHERE THINGS GO
# ---------------
# Output lands in camcheck/ NEXT TO THIS SCRIPT, wherever you put the script.
# Copy cam_check.py into ~/THESIS/Dual_Archi and the images appear in
# ~/THESIS/Dual_Archi/camcheck -- not in some other directory you have to
# remember. "Beside the script" is the least surprising default there is.
#
# Note this is deliberately NOT ~/thesis. capture_gpr.py uses ~/thesis as its
# runtime data directory, and on this Pi ~/thesis and ~/THESIS are two
# different folders (Linux is case-sensitive). Diagnostic images are not
# runtime data, so they do not belong in the same place.
#
# Override, in increasing priority:
#   env CAMCHECK_OUT   / --out-dir DIR
#   env CAMCHECK_ROIS  / --roi-file FILE
SCRIPT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))

OUT_DIR = os.path.expanduser(os.environ.get("CAMCHECK_OUT",
                                            os.path.join(SCRIPT_DIR, "camcheck")))


def _find_rois():
    """pot_rois.json beside the script wins; fall back to capture_gpr.py's
    ~/thesis so this still works if you run the script from elsewhere."""
    env = os.environ.get("CAMCHECK_ROIS")
    if env:
        return os.path.expanduser(env)
    here = os.path.join(SCRIPT_DIR, "pot_rois.json")
    if os.path.exists(here):
        return here
    return os.path.expanduser("~/thesis/pot_rois.json")


ROI_FILE = _find_rois()

# Must match capture_gpr.py EXACTLY, or this test proves nothing about it.
HUE_LO, HUE_HI   = 35, 85          # OpenCV hue units (0-179), NOT degrees
SAT_MIN, VAL_MIN = 40, 40
CAPTURE_SIZE     = (1296, 1296)    # 2x2 binned from 4608x2592

# Camera height above the CANOPY plane, not the floor. LensPosition is in
# dioptres = 1 / (distance in metres). Override with --distance.
DEFAULT_SUBJECT_M = 1.22           # 1650 mm camera - 430 mm canopy top

try:
    import cv2
    import numpy as np
except ImportError:
    sys.exit("opencv missing:  sudo apt install python3-opencv")


def need_picamera2():
    try:
        from picamera2 import Picamera2
        return Picamera2
    except ImportError:
        sys.exit("picamera2 missing. It is installed system-wide by apt:\n"
                 "  sudo apt install python3-picamera2\n"
                 "If you are in a venv it must have been made with "
                 "--system-site-packages.")


def ensure_out():
    """Create the output directory, and fail with a readable message rather
    than a traceback if the path is not writable (a USB stick that is mounted
    read-only, or a folder owned by root, are the usual causes)."""
    try:
        os.makedirs(OUT_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    except OSError as e:
        sys.exit(f"cannot create {OUT_DIR}: {e}\n"
                 f"  pick another with  --out-dir /some/writable/path")
    if not os.access(OUT_DIR, os.W_OK):
        sys.exit(f"{OUT_DIR} is not writable.\n"
                 f"  pick another with  --out-dir /some/writable/path")


# --------------------------------------------------------------------- 1 ---
def cmd_info():
    Picamera2 = need_picamera2()
    cams = Picamera2.global_camera_info()
    if not cams:
        print("NO CAMERA DETECTED.\n"
              "  * check the CSI ribbon at BOTH ends -- contacts face the board\n"
              "  * a Pi Zero 2W needs the narrow-to-standard adapter cable\n"
              "  * libcamera-hello --list-cameras should agree with this")
        return False

    for i, c in enumerate(cams):
        print(f"camera {i}: {c.get('Model','?')}   {c.get('Location','')}")
        print(f"           id: {c.get('Id','?')}")

    model = cams[0].get("Model", "").lower()
    if "imx708" in model:
        print("\n  imx708 = Camera Module 3. HFOV 66 deg, VFOV 41 deg, autofocus.")
        print("  NOTE: your manuscript Section III.B still names the Module V2")
        print("  (VFOV 48.8 deg). Different geometry, different mounting height.")
    elif "imx219" in model:
        print("\n  imx219 = Camera Module V2. No autofocus, VFOV 48.8 deg.")
        print("  If you expected a v3, you have the wrong camera on the rig.")

    picam2 = Picamera2()
    print("\nsensor modes available:")
    for m in picam2.sensor_modes:
        print(f"  {m['size'][0]}x{m['size'][1]}  bit_depth={m.get('bit_depth')}  "
              f"crop_limits={m.get('crop_limits')}")
    print(f"\ncapture_gpr.py asks for {CAPTURE_SIZE[0]}x{CAPTURE_SIZE[1]}.")
    print("If no mode matches that, libcamera will crop or scale -- and a CROP")
    print("means a narrower field of view than your height calculation assumed.")
    picam2.close()
    return True


# --------------------------------------------------------------- helpers ---
# The literal values hardcoded in capture_gpr.py's capture_frame(). Every one
# is marked "MEASURE ONCE" in that file and none has been measured yet. Keep
# these in sync by hand -- --as-gpr is only honest if they match.
GPR_CONTROLS = {
    "LensPosition":  2.5,          # = 40 cm. Almost certainly wrong for the rig.
    "ExposureTime":  8000,         # microseconds = 1/125 s
    "AnalogueGain":  1.0,
    "ColourGains":   (1.8, 1.6),
}


def open_camera(lock=True, lens_position=None, distance_m=DEFAULT_SUBJECT_M,
                exposure_us=None, gain=None, as_gpr=False):
    """Open the camera the way capture_gpr.py does.

    Three lock behaviours, and the difference between them matters:

      lock=False      everything automatic. What your eye would call "correct".
      lock=True       let AE/AWB converge for 2 s, THEN freeze whatever they
                      chose. Stable, and adapted to the light in the room.
      as_gpr=True     force capture_gpr.py's hardcoded ExposureTime/gain. This
                      is what the real pipeline will actually record, and it
                      does NOT adapt to anything.

    lock=True and as_gpr=True give different frames whenever the ambient light
