    differs from what the hardcoded values assume -- which is the whole reason
    --as-gpr exists."""
    Picamera2 = need_picamera2()
    picam2 = Picamera2()
    cfg = picam2.create_still_configuration(
        main={"size": CAPTURE_SIZE, "format": "RGB888"})   # -> BGR array
    picam2.configure(cfg)
    picam2.start()
    time.sleep(2.0)                     # let AE/AWB settle before we freeze them

    if lock or as_gpr:
        from libcamera import controls
        if as_gpr:
            ctrl = {"AfMode": controls.AfModeEnum.Manual,
                    "AeEnable": False, "AwbEnable": False}
            ctrl.update(GPR_CONTROLS)
        else:
            lp = lens_position if lens_position is not None else 1.0 / distance_m
            ctrl = {"AfMode": controls.AfModeEnum.Manual,
                    "LensPosition": lp,
                    "AeEnable": False, "AwbEnable": False}
            if exposure_us is not None:
                ctrl["ExposureTime"] = int(exposure_us)
            if gain is not None:
                ctrl["AnalogueGain"] = float(gain)
        picam2.set_controls(ctrl)
        time.sleep(1.5)
    return picam2


def cmd_as_gpr(args):
    """Show what capture_gpr.py will actually record, right now."""
    print("CAPTURE USING capture_gpr.py's HARDCODED CONTROLS")
    for k, v in GPR_CONTROLS.items():
        print(f"    {k:<14} {v}")
    print("\n  Every one of these is marked 'MEASURE ONCE' in capture_gpr.py.")
    print("  None has been measured. This is what the rig would record today.\n")

    picam2 = open_camera(as_gpr=True)
    try:
        bgr = picam2.capture_array()
        st, g = report_frame(bgr, "as_gpr")
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()

    lp = GPR_CONTROLS["LensPosition"]
    print(f"\n  LensPosition {lp} focuses at {1.0/lp:.2f} m.")
    print(f"  Your camera sits about {args.distance:.2f} m from the canopy.")
    if abs(1.0 / lp - args.distance) > 0.25:
        print("  ! These disagree. The frame above is out of focus by design.")
        print("    Run --focus-sweep and put the result into capture_gpr.py.")


def cmd_exposure_sweep(args):
    """Find the ExposureTime to hardcode, the same way --focus-sweep finds
    LensPosition. Run this at the hour the cron job runs -- noon."""
    steps = [2000, 4000, 8000, 12000, 16000, 24000, 33000, 50000, 75000]
    print("EXPOSURE SWEEP -- run this AT NOON, under the light the rig will")
    print("actually have. An exposure chosen at dusk is wrong by 5-6 stops.\n")
    print(f"  (capture_gpr.py currently hardcodes {GPR_CONTROLS['ExposureTime']} us)\n")

    picam2 = open_camera(lock=True, distance_m=args.distance,
                         exposure_us=steps[0], gain=1.0)
    ensure_out()
    rows = []
    try:
        print("  exposure   shutter    mean   blown%   crushed%   green%")
        for us in steps:
            picam2.set_controls({"ExposureTime": int(us), "AnalogueGain": 1.0})
            time.sleep(1.2)
            bgr = picam2.capture_array()
            st = exposure_stats(bgr)
            g, _ = green_fraction(bgr)
            rows.append((us, st, g))
            print(f"  {us:>7} us  1/{1000000//us:<6} {st['mean']:7.1f} "
                  f"{st['clip_hi']:7.2f} {st['clip_lo']:9.2f} {g:8.2f}")
            cv2.imwrite(os.path.join(OUT_DIR, f"exp_{us}.jpg"), bgr)
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()

    ok = [r for r in rows
          if 80 <= r[1]["mean"] <= 180 and r[1]["clip_hi"] < 1.0 and r[1]["clip_lo"] < 1.0]
    print()
    if ok:
        best = min(ok, key=lambda r: abs(r[1]["mean"] - 130))
        print(f"  best: ExposureTime = {best[0]} us   (mean {best[1]['mean']:.1f}, "
              f"green {best[2]:.2f} %)")
        print(f"\n  Put this in capture_gpr.py:   \"ExposureTime\": {best[0]},")
    else:
        print("  NOTHING in this range lands in the target window.")
        print("  Too dark everywhere -> add light, or raise AnalogueGain (adds noise).")
        print("  Too bright everywhere -> the scene is brighter than a 1/500 s")
        print("  shutter can handle; shade it or extend the sweep downward.")
    print(f"\n  Frames in {OUT_DIR}/exp_*.jpg -- confirm by eye.")
    print("  Watch the green% column: if it swings with exposure, your GPR is")
    print("  sensitive to lighting and the lock is doing real work.")


def sharpness(gray):
    """Variance of the Laplacian. Higher = crisper edges.

    The ABSOLUTE number is scene-dependent and means nothing on its own. It is
    only meaningful COMPARED between shots of the SAME scene -- which is exactly
    what --focus-sweep does."""
    return float(cv2.Laplacian(gray, cv2.CV_64F).var())


def exposure_stats(bgr):
    gray = cv2.cvtColor(bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY)
    total = gray.size
    return {
        "mean":      float(gray.mean()),
        "clip_hi":   100.0 * int((gray >= 250).sum()) / total,
        "clip_lo":   100.0 * int((gray <= 5).sum()) / total,
        "sharpness": sharpness(gray),
    }


def green_fraction(bgr):
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
    m = cv2.inRange(hsv,
                    np.array([HUE_LO, SAT_MIN, VAL_MIN], np.uint8),
                    np.array([HUE_HI, 255, 255], np.uint8))
    return 100.0 * int(np.count_nonzero(m)) / m.size, m


def report_frame(bgr, tag):
    ensure_out()
    st = exposure_stats(bgr)
    gpct, mask = green_fraction(bgr)
    h, w = bgr.shape[:2]

    img_p  = os.path.join(OUT_DIR, f"{tag}.jpg")
    mask_p = os.path.join(OUT_DIR, f"{tag}_mask.png")
    cv2.imwrite(img_p, bgr)
    cv2.imwrite(mask_p, mask)

    print(f"\n  resolution     {w} x {h}")
    if (w, h) != CAPTURE_SIZE:
        print(f"    !! capture_gpr.py expects {CAPTURE_SIZE[0]}x{CAPTURE_SIZE[1]}. "
              f"ROI pixel coordinates will not line up.")
    print(f"  mean brightness{st['mean']:8.1f}      (want roughly 80-180)")
    print(f"  blown-out      {st['clip_hi']:8.2f} %    (want < 1 %)")
    print(f"  crushed black  {st['clip_lo']:8.2f} %    (want < 1 %)")
    print(f"  sharpness      {st['sharpness']:8.1f}      (compare, never absolute)")
    print(f"  green pixels   {gpct:8.2f} %    HSV H{HUE_LO}-{HUE_HI} S>{SAT_MIN} V>{VAL_MIN}")
    print(f"\n  saved  {img_p}")
    print(f"  saved  {mask_p}   <- OPEN THIS. Leaves white, everything else black.")

    if st["clip_hi"] > 1.0:
        print("\n  ! blown highlights. Leaves that clip to white lose saturation and")
        print("    drop out of the HSV band -- GPR reads LOW in bright sun.")
    if st["mean"] < 60:
        print("\n  ! dark frame. V>40 will reject shadowed leaf and GPR reads low.")
    return st, gpct


# --------------------------------------------------------------------- 2 ---
def cmd_capture(args):
    picam2 = open_camera(lock=not args.auto, distance_m=args.distance)
    try:
        bgr = picam2.capture_array()
        print("SINGLE CAPTURE" + ("  (auto exposure/focus)" if args.auto
                                  else "  (locked, as capture_gpr.py runs it)"))
        report_frame(bgr, "capture")
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()


# --------------------------------------------------------------------- 3 ---
def cmd_focus_sweep(args):
    """Autofocus MUST be locked for a 35-day series -- if focus hunts between
    days, edge sharpness changes and the green pixel count drifts with no
    change in the plant. This finds the value to lock it AT."""
    Picamera2 = need_picamera2()
    cams = Picamera2.global_camera_info()
    if cams and "imx708" not in cams[0].get("Model", "").lower():
        print("This camera has no autofocus -- nothing to sweep. Focus is fixed.")
        return

    nominal = 1.0 / args.distance
    positions = [round(nominal + d, 2) for d in
                 (-0.40, -0.25, -0.15, -0.05, 0.0, 0.05, 0.15, 0.25, 0.40)]
    positions = [p for p in positions if p >= 0.0]

    print(f"FOCUS SWEEP around {nominal:.2f} dioptres "
          f"(= {args.distance:.2f} m to the canopy)\n")
    print("  Put a leaf, or a printed page of text, at the CANOPY plane first --")
    print("  not on the floor. Focusing on the floor leaves the leaves soft.\n")

    picam2 = open_camera(lock=True, lens_position=positions[0],
                         distance_m=args.distance)
    ensure_out()
    results = []
    try:
        print("  LensPosition   distance     sharpness")
        for lp in positions:
            picam2.set_controls({"LensPosition": lp})
            time.sleep(1.2)
            bgr = picam2.capture_array()
            g = cv2.cvtColor(bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2GRAY)
            s = sharpness(g)
            results.append((lp, s))
            dist = ("inf" if lp <= 0.01 else f"{1.0/lp:.2f} m")
            print(f"  {lp:>10.2f}   {dist:>8}   {s:10.1f}")
            cv2.imwrite(os.path.join(OUT_DIR, f"focus_{lp:.2f}.jpg"), bgr)
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()

    best = max(results, key=lambda r: r[1])
    print(f"\n  sharpest: LensPosition = {best[0]:.2f}  (score {best[1]:.1f})")
    if best[0] in (positions[0], positions[-1]):
        print("  ! the best value is at the EDGE of the sweep -- the true optimum is")
        print("    outside it. Re-run with a --distance closer to reality.")
    print(f"\n  Put this in capture_gpr.py:   \"LensPosition\": {best[0]:.2f}")
    print(f"  Frames are in {OUT_DIR}/focus_*.jpg -- confirm by eye before trusting")
    print("  the number. Laplacian variance also rewards noise and moving leaves.")


# --------------------------------------------------------------------- 4 ---
def cmd_rois(args):
    if not os.path.exists(ROI_FILE):
        print(f"no {ROI_FILE} -- run make_rois.py first.")
        return
    with open(ROI_FILE) as f:
        cfg = json.load(f)
    rois = cfg.get("rois", cfg)
    circular = bool(cfg.get("circular", True))

    picam2 = open_camera(lock=True, distance_m=args.distance)
    try:
        bgr = picam2.capture_array()
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()

    h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
    gpct, mask = green_fraction(bgr)
    out = bgr.copy()
    problems = []

    print(f"\n  frame {w}x{h}   ROI file: {len(rois)} entries, "
          f"circular={circular}\n")
    print("  pot     inside frame     green in ROI")
    for i in range(9):
        pid = f"P-0{i+1}"
        r = rois.get(pid)
        if not r:
            problems.append(f"{pid} missing from {ROI_FILE}")
            continue
        x, y, ww, hh = r["x"], r["y"], r["w"], r["h"]
        inside = (x >= 0 and y >= 0 and x + ww <= w and y + hh <= h)

        area = np.zeros((h, w), np.uint8)
        if circular:
            cx, cy = x + ww // 2, y + hh // 2
            rad = min(ww, hh) // 2
            cv2.circle(area, (cx, cy), rad, 255, -1)
            cv2.circle(out, (cx, cy), rad, (0, 255, 0) if inside else (0, 0, 255), 3)
        else:
            area[max(0, y):y + hh, max(0, x):x + ww] = 255
            cv2.rectangle(out, (x, y), (x + ww, y + hh),
                          (0, 255, 0) if inside else (0, 0, 255), 3)

        tot = int(np.count_nonzero(area))
        grn = int(np.count_nonzero(cv2.bitwise_and(mask, mask, mask=area)))
        pct = 100.0 * grn / tot if tot else 0.0
        cv2.putText(out, pid, (x, max(20, y - 8)),
                    cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.9,
                    (0, 255, 0) if inside else (0, 0, 255), 2)

        print(f"  {pid}   {'yes' if inside else 'NO -- CLIPPED':<16} {pct:6.2f} %")
        if not inside:
            problems.append(f"{pid} extends outside the frame")

    ensure_out()
    p = os.path.join(OUT_DIR, "rois.jpg")
    cv2.imwrite(p, out)
    print(f"\n  saved  {p}   <- open it, check every circle sits on a pot rim")

    if problems:
        print("\n  PROBLEMS:")
        for x in problems:
            print(f"    * {x}")
        print("\n  A clipped ROI does not error. It reads a smaller area and that")
        print("  pot's GPR is wrong for all 35 days. Raise the camera or re-run")
        print("  make_rois.py against THIS frame.")
    else:
        print("\n  all nine ROIs are fully inside the frame.")


# --------------------------------------------------------------------- 5 ---
def cmd_check(args):
    print("=" * 62)
    print("CAMERA ACCEPTANCE CHECK")
    print("=" * 62)
    if not cmd_info():
        return
    print("\n" + "-" * 62)
    picam2 = open_camera(lock=True, distance_m=args.distance)
    try:
        print("\nLOCKED CAPTURE (exactly as capture_gpr.py runs it)")
        bgr = picam2.capture_array()
        st1, g1 = report_frame(bgr, "check_1")

        print("\n" + "-" * 62)
        print("REPEATABILITY -- two frames 5 s apart, nothing moving.")
        print("Locked controls mean these should be nearly identical. Drift here")
        print("is drift that will show up as fake growth in your time series.")
        time.sleep(5)
        bgr2 = picam2.capture_array()
        st2, g2 = report_frame(bgr2, "check_2")

        dg = abs(g2 - g1)
        db = abs(st2["mean"] - st1["mean"])
        print(f"\n  green delta      {dg:6.3f} pp   (want < 0.10)")
        print(f"  brightness delta {db:6.2f}     (want < 2.0)")
        if dg > 0.10 or db > 2.0:
            print("\n  ! NOT STABLE. Something is still auto-adjusting, or the light")
            print("    is changing. Confirm AeEnable/AwbEnable are False and AfMode")
            print("    is Manual. An unstable camera makes GPR unusable.")
        else:
            print("\n  stable.")
    finally:
        picam2.stop(); picam2.close()

    print("\n" + "-" * 62)
    cmd_rois(args)

    print("\n" + "=" * 62)
    print("Open every file in " + OUT_DIR + " before you call this passed.")
    print("The numbers tell you where to look. Your eyes decide.")
    print("=" * 62)


def main():
    global OUT_DIR, ROI_FILE
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Group B camera acceptance test")
    ap.add_argument("--info",        action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--capture",     action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--focus-sweep", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--rois",        action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--check",       action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--as-gpr",      action="store_true",
                    help="capture with capture_gpr.py's hardcoded controls")
    ap.add_argument("--exposure-sweep", action="store_true",
                    help="find the ExposureTime to hardcode. RUN AT NOON.")
    ap.add_argument("--auto",        action="store_true",
                    help="--capture with auto exposure/focus, for comparison")
    ap.add_argument("--distance", type=float, default=DEFAULT_SUBJECT_M,
                    help="metres from lens to CANOPY plane (default %.2f)"
                         % DEFAULT_SUBJECT_M)
    ap.add_argument("--out-dir", default=None, metavar="DIR",
                    help="where to save images (default %s). Created if needed."
                         % OUT_DIR)
    ap.add_argument("--roi-file", default=None, metavar="FILE",
                    help="pot_rois.json to check against (default %s)" % ROI_FILE)
    a = ap.parse_args()

    if a.out_dir:
        OUT_DIR = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(a.out_dir))
    if a.roi_file:
        ROI_FILE = os.path.abspath(os.path.expanduser(a.roi_file))
    print(f"[cam_check] images -> {OUT_DIR}")

    if   a.info:           cmd_info()
    elif a.as_gpr:         cmd_as_gpr(a)
    elif a.exposure_sweep: cmd_exposure_sweep(a)
    elif a.capture:     cmd_capture(a)
    elif a.focus_sweep: cmd_focus_sweep(a)
    elif a.rois:        cmd_rois(a)
    elif a.check:       cmd_check(a)
    else:
        ap.print_help()
        print("\nStart with:  python3 cam_check.py --info")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\capture_gpr.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
capture_gpr.py -- daily noon vision pipeline on the Raspberry Pi Zero 2W.  v5

Thesis: Computer Vision-Based Growth Monitoring and Yield Prediction for
        Pechay Under Differential Soil Moisture Treatments in a Greenhouse

Implements Appendix G. One frame at 12:00, HSV threshold inside each pot's ROI,
Green Pixel Ratio per pot, appended to growth_dataset.csv together with the soil
moisture pulled from the ESP32-S3. From D12 (2026-10-17) the raw noon frame is
also kept as a lossless PNG in ~/agrisentry_data/frames/ (~3 MB/day), so the GPR
can be recomputed if the ROIs or mask ever change; before that it was discarded.

Deliverable 3 (per-pot GPR time-series) and Deliverable 5 (the SM column of the
training dataset, 9 pots x D8-D30 = 207 rows) both come out of this script.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
CHANGES FROM v4 -- read these before running
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1. SYNTAX FIX. v4 had a stray shell command ("sh ced@10.195...") pasted between
    validate_rois() and main(). The file would not even parse.

 2. MORPH_K: 9 -> 3.  The v4 comment claimed "a 5x5 opening only removes bridges
    up to 2 px wide -- a 3 px bridge survives it intact". That was measured and
    is FALSE. A k x k opening destroys anything NARROWER than k px:

        bridge  3x3   5x5   7x7   9x9
          2px   GONE  GONE  GONE  GONE
          3px   ok    GONE  GONE  GONE
          5px   ok    ok    GONE  GONE
          9px   ok    ok    ok    ok

    Measured against the real D=5 seedling photo (test_2.png), fraction of
    genuine green pixels retained:  3x3 = 98.4%,  5x5 = 95.4%,  9x9 = 75.0%.
    At 9x9 pot P-05 read 0.000% for a seedling plainly visible in the frame.
    At 1.34 px/mm, 9x9 erases anything thinner than 6.7 mm -- wider than a
    pechay cotyledon. It is sized to delete the plant during weeks 1-2.

    Note also that cross-pot leaf bridges are not inside ANY ROI: the ROI is
    90% of the rim and there is ~130 mm of bare soil between rims at 330 mm
    pitch. The guard margin already does the job Section III.C credits to the
    opening. The kernel's remaining useful role is killing speckle noise.

 3. SOWING_DATE is now read from ~/thesis/sowing_date.txt when present, and
    day_index() refuses to run outside D = 1..35. v4 shipped a hardcoded past
    date with no bounds check -- running it before sowing wrote a wrong D, and
    D is a term in the model.

 4. DUPLICATE-DAY GUARD. v4 appended unconditionally, so a second run in one day
    silently wrote 18 rows instead of 9. Duplicated plant-days over-weight that
    day in every LOOCV fold it appears in, inflating RÂ². Now it refuses.

 5. pi_link is imported lazily, so --no-sm --test-image works on a machine with
    no pyserial installed.

 6. Camera height. v4's docstring said 1350 mm for a 900 mm array, measured to
    the floor. Both were wrong. Clearance must be measured to the CANOPY plane
    (pot height + plant height ~= 430 mm), not the floor, because the canopy is
    closer to the lens and clips first. For the real 860 mm pot bounding box at
    330 mm pitch, the v3's 41 deg vertical FOV needs the camera at 1600-1650 mm
    above the floor. Section III.B still needs updating -- it also still names
    the Camera Module V2 (48.8 deg VFOV), which is not the camera in use.

 7. NEW DIAGNOSTIC COLUMNS (GPR itself is unchanged -- these are additions):
      raw_gpr_noopen  same GPR with the opening skipped. Run both for the first
                      week; if they track, the kernel is not eating your plants.
      terr_gpr        green fraction inside a TERRITORY circle (the pot's
                      Voronoi cell at your pitch) instead of the pot rim. The
                      rim ROI saturates once canopy diameter exceeds ~180 mm,
                      which happens around D=24-28 while fresh weight is still
                      climbing. terr_gpr keeps rising. Costs one extra mask.
      canopy_w_px/h_px  bounding box of the largest green blob in the ROI. Keeps
                      growing after area saturates.

 8. validate_rois() now does a circle-distance check when circular=True. v4
    tested bounding boxes, which can overlap while the circles do not -- it
    could refuse a perfectly good ROI file on planting day.

 9. The anomaly flag now scales with the gap between readings. v4 compared to
    the previous ROW regardless of how many days had passed, so one missed
    capture guaranteed a false flag during the fast-growth window.

10. Debug overlay draws circles when circular=True (v4 always drew rectangles,
    which did not show the ROI actually being measured).

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Install (Bookworm):
    python3 -m venv --system-site-packages ~/thesis-env
    source ~/thesis-env/bin/activate
    pip install opencv-python-headless numpy pyserial

  --system-site-packages is REQUIRED. Picamera2 is installed system-wide by apt
  and cannot be pip-installed into an isolated venv.

Cron (crontab -e).  flock stops a hung run from overlapping tomorrow's:
    0 12 * * * /usr/bin/flock -n /tmp/gpr.lock /home/USER/thesis-env/bin/python3 \
               /home/USER/thesis/capture_gpr.py >> /home/USER/thesis/cron.log 2>&1

  Check `whoami` -- Bookworm's default user is usually NOT "pi".
  Set the timezone ONCE before D=1 and never change it mid-run:
      sudo timedatectl set-timezone Asia/Manila

Offline testing, no camera and no ESP32 required:
    python3 capture_gpr.py --test-image frame.jpg --no-write --no-sm
    python3 capture_gpr.py --test-image frame.jpg --debug-overlay /tmp/ov.png

CAMERA MODULE 3 -- autofocus MUST be locked. If focus hunts between days, edge
sharpness changes and the green pixel count drifts independently of growth.
"""

import argparse
import csv
import json
import os
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from datetime import datetime

import cv2
import numpy as np

BASE = os.path.expanduser("~/thesis")
ROI_FILE = os.path.join(BASE, "pot_rois.json")
OUT_CSV = os.path.join(BASE, "growth_dataset.csv")
SOWING_FILE = os.path.join(BASE, "sowing_date.txt")
FRAME_DIR = os.path.expanduser("~/agrisentry_data/frames")   # daily raw noon frames

# Fallback only. Prefer writing the real date into ~/thesis/sowing_date.txt on
# sowing day so nobody has to edit source on a live rig.
SOWING_DATE_FALLBACK = "2026-08-15"
CYCLE_DAYS = 30          # grow-out shortened to 30 days (harvest on D30)

CAPTURE_SIZE = (1296, 1296)                  # 2x binned from 4608x2592

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# RIG GEOMETRY -- measure these on the finished rig
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PX_PER_MM: photograph a ruler lying at the POT RIM plane and count.
# At 1650 mm camera height over an 860 mm pot box this comes out near 1.34.
PX_PER_MM = 1.34
POT_PITCH_MM = 330.0        # centre-to-centre. Territory circle diameter.

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# HSV THRESHOLD
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The manuscript writes the mask as "35 deg <= Hue <= 85 deg". Those NUMBERS
# are correct, but the degree symbols are not: 35 and 85 are OpenCV hue units,
# not degrees. OpenCV stores hue on a 0-179 scale, so 35-85 spans 70-170 deg on
# the true colour wheel, which is exactly where leaf green lives.
#
# VERIFIED on a real outdoor foliage frame: accepted pixels had median hue 67,
# 5th-95th percentile 43-82. Comfortably inside the band.
#
# Do NOT "convert" 35-85 to 18-42 by halving. That returns 0.00 % GPR on a
# healthy green canopy, because it selects orange-yellow instead of green.
# Keep these values in OpenCV units. Fix the manuscript text, not the code.
HUE_LO, HUE_HI = 38, 85
SAT_MIN, VAL_MIN = 100, 55      # Filters out dry soil and shade cloth glare

# Opening kernel, in PIXELS but chosen from a PHYSICAL size. A k x k opening
# erases anything thinner than k px. At PX_PER_MM = 1.34, k = 3 erases features
# thinner than 2.2 mm -- below a pechay petiole, above single-pixel noise.
# Raising this deletes real plant. See CHANGES note 2.
MORPH_MM = 2.2               # thinnest real feature we are willing to lose
MORPH_K = max(3, int(round(MORPH_MM * PX_PER_MM)) | 1)   # odd, never below 3
MORPH_KERNEL = np.ones((MORPH_K, MORPH_K), np.uint8)

# GPR jumping more than this PER DAY is implausible and usually means the rig
# moved or the lighting changed. Scaled by the gap between readings so a missed
# capture does not auto-flag the next one. Flagged, not discarded.
ANOMALY_DELTA_PER_DAY = 8.0

CSV_HEADER = ["day", "timestamp", "pot_id", "sm_pct",
              "raw_gpr", "smooth_gpr", "green_px", "total_px", "flag",
              "raw_gpr_noopen", "terr_gpr", "terr_green_px", "terr_total_px",
              "canopy_w_px", "canopy_h_px", "morph_k",
              "cell_gpr", "cell_green_px", "cell_total_px"]

# Pot-area ("cell") GPR, live from run 2 (sown 2026-10-29). Each pot owns the
# pixels closer to its centre than to any other pot's centre, up to CELL_R_PX.
# Cells never overlap, and a plant leaning past its rim stays in its own cell
# (run 1: the pot circle read 0 for leaning P-01/P-05/P-07).
CELL_R_PX = 175          # ~13 cm at 1.34 px/mm; half the closest pot spacing is ~160-180 px


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# DAY INDEX
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def sowing_date():
    """Read the sowing date from a file so nobody edits source on a live rig."""
    if os.path.exists(SOWING_FILE):
        with open(SOWING_FILE) as f:
            txt = f.read().strip()
        if txt:
            return txt
    return SOWING_DATE_FALLBACK


def day_index(allow_any=False):
    """D, with D = 1 the sowing date. Refuses to run outside the cycle.

    D is a term in the regression model. A wrong D is not recoverable after the
    fact, and the CSV looks perfectly well-formed either way -- so fail loudly."""
    sown_txt = sowing_date()
    try:
        sown = datetime.strptime(sown_txt, "%Y-%m-%d").date()
    except ValueError:
        sys.exit(f"[capture_gpr] bad sowing date {sown_txt!r} "
                 f"(want YYYY-MM-DD, from {SOWING_FILE} or the fallback)")
    d = (datetime.now().date() - sown).days + 1
    if not allow_any and not 1 <= d <= CYCLE_DAYS:
        sys.exit(f"[capture_gpr] D={d} is outside the 1..{CYCLE_DAYS} cycle "
                 f"(sowing date {sown_txt}) -- refusing to write.\n"
                 f"  If sowing has happened, put the real date in {SOWING_FILE}.\n"
                 f"  For a deliberate out-of-cycle test, pass --allow-any-day.")
    return d


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CAPTURE
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Capture light (relay 12). When USE_CAPTURE_LIGHT is True it is switched on for
# every capture so illumination is identical day to day. OFF since 2026-10-12:
# the light plus camera load was suspected of browning out the Pi, and noon
# daylight is used instead. Flip back to True once the light is proven safe.
USE_CAPTURE_LIGHT = False
# The ESP32 turns it off by itself after LIGHT_PULSE_MS even if this
# script dies; we also switch it off explicitly as soon as the frame is taken.
LIGHT_PULSE_MS = 20000

# Brightness normalisation (since 2026-10-14). One fixed exposure fails across
# weather: 24 ms washed seedlings out on a sunny noon (Oct 13), 6 ms left them too
# dark to pass V >= VAL_MIN on an overcast noon (Oct 14, mean V 13 vs 73). So each
# capture starts at 6 ms and rescales the exposure until the frame's mean V matches
# the calibrated reference frame (Oct 13 12:00, 6 ms, mean V = 73). Gain and white
# balance stay locked, so colour/hue are unchanged -- only brightness is matched.
# The exposure used is logged to exposure_log.csv every day.
AUTO_EXPOSURE = True
TARGET_MEAN_V = 73.0
EXPOSURE_START_US = 6000
EXPOSURE_MIN_US, EXPOSURE_MAX_US = 1000, 100000
LAST_EXPOSURE = {}          # filled by capture_frame(): exposure_us, mean_v, steps
EXPOSURE_LOG = os.path.join(BASE, "exposure_log.csv")

# Light only when it is really dark: if even the longest exposure cannot bring the
# frame within 20 % of the target brightness, the capture light is switched on and
# the brightness is matched again. Light days are marked light_used=1 in
# exposure_log.csv, because lit frames read higher GPR (more saturated green) --
# flag/check those days in the analysis.
AUTO_LIGHT_WHEN_DARK = True
DARK_V = 0.8 * TARGET_MEAN_V
LIGHT_SETTLE_S = 1.5        # let the light reach full, steady output first


def _light(on):
    """Capture light on (as a self-expiring pulse) or off. Returns success."""
    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    import pi_link                                # lazy: needs pyserial
    ok = pi_link.light_pulse(LIGHT_PULSE_MS) if on else pi_link.set_light_state(False)
    if not ok and on:                             # one retry before giving up
        time.sleep(0.5)
        ok = pi_link.light_pulse(LIGHT_PULSE_MS)
    return ok


def open_locked_camera():
    """Started Picamera2 with the exact configuration and locked controls used
    for every GPR capture. live_feed.py uses this too, so what the alignment
    stream shows is framed, focused and exposed like the real capture.

    Locking matters more than it looks. The methodology treats lighting as a
    strict control variable, but auto-exposure and auto-white-balance actively
    fight that: if the camera compensates for a duller day the HSV values shift
    and the GPR moves without the plant changing."""
    from picamera2 import Picamera2
    from libcamera import controls

    cam = Picamera2()
    cfg = cam.create_still_configuration(main={"size": CAPTURE_SIZE})
    cam.configure(cfg)
    cam.start()
    cam.set_controls({
        "AfMode": controls.AfModeEnum.Manual,
        "LensPosition": 0.42,
        "AeEnable": False,
        "ExposureTime": 6000,       # 6 ms. Sweep 2026-10-13 11:54 (noon daylight, no capture
                                    # light): 24 ms washed seedlings out (57 green px total);
                                    # 4-8 ms all ~1500 px, seedlings only, <1% clipped
        "AnalogueGain": 1.0,         # Minimum gain
        "AwbEnable": False,
        "ColourGains": (1.8, 1.6),
    })
    return cam


def _mean_v(frame):
    small = cv2.resize(frame, (324, 324), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
    # capture_array() gives RGB here; V (= max channel) is the same either way
    return float(small.max(axis=2).mean())


def _match_brightness(cam, frame):
    """Rescale ExposureTime until mean V is within 5 % of TARGET_MEAN_V."""
    exp = EXPOSURE_START_US
    v = _mean_v(frame)
    steps = []
    for _ in range(5):
        steps.append((exp, round(v, 1)))
        if abs(v - TARGET_MEAN_V) <= 0.05 * TARGET_MEAN_V or v <= 0:
            break
        new = int(min(max(exp * TARGET_MEAN_V / v, EXPOSURE_MIN_US), EXPOSURE_MAX_US))
        if new == exp:
            break
        exp = new
        cam.set_controls({"ExposureTime": exp})
        time.sleep(0.6)
        cam.capture_array()            # flush a frame taken with the old setting
        frame = cam.capture_array()
        v = _mean_v(frame)
    LAST_EXPOSURE.update(exposure_us=exp, mean_v=round(v, 1), steps=steps)  # keeps light_used
    print(f"[capture_gpr] exposure {exp / 1000:.1f} ms -> mean V {v:.1f} "
          f"(target {TARGET_MEAN_V:.0f}; steps {steps})")
    return frame


def capture_frame(use_light=USE_CAPTURE_LIGHT):
    """One stabilised frame from open_locked_camera(), under the capture light.

    Picamera2's format names are inverted -- the default still config hands back
    an array OpenCV can use directly. Verify once with a known-red object: if it
    photographs as blue, drop the cvtColor below."""
    if use_light:
        if _light(True):
            print("[capture_gpr] capture light ON")
            time.sleep(LIGHT_SETTLE_S)
        else:
            print("[capture_gpr] WARNING: capture light did NOT switch on -- this "
                  "frame is lit differently from the others", file=sys.stderr)

    light_on = use_light
    LAST_EXPOSURE["light_used"] = int(bool(use_light))
    try:
        cam = open_locked_camera()
        time.sleep(2)                   # let the locked settings take effect
        frame = cam.capture_array()
        if AUTO_EXPOSURE:
            frame = _match_brightness(cam, frame)
            too_dark = (LAST_EXPOSURE["exposure_us"] >= EXPOSURE_MAX_US
                        and LAST_EXPOSURE["mean_v"] < DARK_V)
            if too_dark and AUTO_LIGHT_WHEN_DARK and not light_on:
                print(f"[capture_gpr] too dark even at {EXPOSURE_MAX_US / 1000:.0f} ms "
                      f"(mean V {LAST_EXPOSURE['mean_v']}) -- switching the capture light on")
                if _light(True):
                    light_on = True
                    LAST_EXPOSURE["light_used"] = 1
                    time.sleep(LIGHT_SETTLE_S)
                    cam.set_controls({"ExposureTime": EXPOSURE_START_US})
                    time.sleep(0.6)
                    cam.capture_array()
                    frame = _match_brightness(cam, cam.capture_array())
                else:
                    print("[capture_gpr] WARNING: capture light did NOT switch on",
                          file=sys.stderr)
        cam.stop()
        cam.close()
    finally:
        if light_on:
            _light(False)
            print("[capture_gpr] capture light OFF")
    return cv2.cvtColor(frame, cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MASKS
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def circle_mask(shape_hw, cx, cy, r):
    m = np.zeros(shape_hw, np.uint8)
    cv2.circle(m, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(r), 255, -1)
    return m


def roi_geometry(roi):
    """Centre and radius of one pot ROI, in pixels."""
    cx = roi["x"] + roi["w"] / 2.0
    cy = roi["y"] + roi["h"] / 2.0
    r = min(roi["w"], roi["h"]) / 2.0
    return cx, cy, r


def roi_mask(shape_hw, roi, circular):
    """Pixels belonging to this pot.

    A square ROI drawn around a circular pot puts ~21.5 % bench and rim inside
    the denominator, so 'GPR' stops being the fraction of POT area covered and
    becomes the fraction of a rectangle. Circular is the truer denominator and
    is the default; set "circular": false in pot_rois.json to fall back."""
    if circular:
        cx, cy, r = roi_geometry(roi)
        return circle_mask(shape_hw, cx, cy, r)
    m = np.zeros(shape_hw, np.uint8)
    m[roi["y"]:roi["y"] + roi["h"], roi["x"]:roi["x"] + roi["w"]] = 255
    return m


def hsv_mask(hsv):
    """Raw HSV threshold, no morphology."""
    return cv2.inRange(hsv,
                       np.array([HUE_LO, SAT_MIN, VAL_MIN], np.uint8),
                       np.array([HUE_HI, 255, 255], np.uint8))


def green_mask(hsv):
    """Threshold + opening for the WHOLE frame, computed once.

    Hoisted out of the per-pot loop: on a Zero 2W the opening is the single most
    expensive step, and running it nine times over the full 2304x1296 frame was
    ~9x more work than needed. Opening the whole frame is also slightly more
    correct -- per-ROI opening would treat each ROI edge as a boundary."""
    return cv2.morphologyEx(hsv_mask(hsv), cv2.MORPH_OPEN, MORPH_KERNEL)


def cell_masks(shape_hw, rois, r_px=CELL_R_PX):
    """{pot_id: uint8 mask} -- each pot's Voronoi cell, capped at r_px.

    Running min over the 9 centres in float32, so it stays small on the Zero 2W
    (a full 9-layer distance stack would be ~120 MB)."""
    h, w = shape_hw
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:h, 0:w].astype(np.float32)
    best = np.full((h, w), np.inf, np.float32)
    owner = np.full((h, w), -1, np.int8)
    ids = sorted(rois)
    for i, pid in enumerate(ids):
        cx, cy, _ = roi_geometry(rois[pid])
        d2 = (xx - cx) ** 2 + (yy - cy) ** 2
        closer = d2 < best
        best[closer] = d2[closer]
        owner[closer] = i
    owner[best > r_px * r_px] = -1
    return {pid: ((owner == i).astype(np.uint8) * 255) for i, pid in enumerate(ids)}


def masked_count(mask, area):
    return int(np.count_nonzero(cv2.bitwise_and(mask, mask, mask=area)))


def canopy_bbox(mask, area):
    """Bounding box of the largest green blob inside `area`, in pixels.

    Keeps growing after GPR saturates, so it survives the ceiling effect."""
    sub = cv2.bitwise_and(mask, mask, mask=area)
    n, _, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(sub, connectivity=8)
    if n <= 1:
        return 0, 0
    i = 1 + int(np.argmax(stats[1:, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA]))
    return int(stats[i, cv2.CC_STAT_WIDTH]), int(stats[i, cv2.CC_STAT_HEIGHT])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# HISTORY
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def previous_gpr_all():
    """{pot_id: (day, raw_gpr)} for the most recent row of each pot."""
    if not os.path.exists(OUT_CSV):
        return {}
    out = {}
    with open(OUT_CSV, newline="") as f:
        for row in csv.DictReader(f):
            try:
                out[row["pot_id"]] = (int(row["day"]), float(row["raw_gpr"]))
            except (KeyError, ValueError, TypeError):
                continue
    return out


def already_written(d):
    """True if day d is already in the CSV.

    Without this, a second run in one day silently appends 9 more rows. The
    duplicate plant-days then over-weight that day in every LOOCV fold they
    appear in, inflating RÂ² and shrinking MAE -- and the CSV looks fine."""
    if not os.path.exists(OUT_CSV):
        return False
    with open(OUT_CSV, newline="") as f:
        return any(r.get("day") == str(d) for r in csv.DictReader(f))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# VALIDATION
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def validate_rois(rois, shape_hw, circular):
    """Refuse to run on ROIs that fall outside the frame or genuinely overlap.

    Cheap insurance: a bad pot_rois.json silently produces plausible-looking
    numbers for 35 days, and there is no way to recover the run afterwards."""
    h, w = shape_hw
    problems = []
    present = {}
    for pid in [f"P-0{i}" for i in range(1, 10)]:
        r = rois.get(pid)
        if r is None:
            problems.append(f"{pid}: missing from pot_rois.json")
            continue
        if r["w"] <= 0 or r["h"] <= 0:
            problems.append(f"{pid}: non-positive size {r['w']}x{r['h']}")
            continue
        if r["x"] < 0 or r["y"] < 0 or r["x"] + r["w"] > w or r["y"] + r["h"] > h:
            problems.append(f"{pid}: box {r['x']},{r['y']} {r['w']}x{r['h']} "
                            f"falls outside the {w}x{h} frame")
        present[pid] = r

    ids = list(present)
    for i in range(len(ids)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(ids)):
            a, b = present[ids[i]], present[ids[j]]
            if circular:
                # circle-distance: two boxes can overlap while the inscribed
                # circles do not, and refusing that would be a false alarm
                ax, ay, ar = roi_geometry(a)
                bx, by, br = roi_geometry(b)
                if math_hypot(ax - bx, ay - by) < ar + br:
                    problems.append(f"{ids[i]} and {ids[j]} circles overlap")
            elif (a["x"] < b["x"] + b["w"] and b["x"] < a["x"] + a["w"] and
                  a["y"] < b["y"] + b["h"] and b["y"] < a["y"] + a["h"]):
                problems.append(f"{ids[i]} and {ids[j]} overlap")
    return problems


def math_hypot(dx, dy):
    return (dx * dx + dy * dy) ** 0.5


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Daily noon GPR capture")
    ap.add_argument("--test-image", help="use this image instead of the camera")
    ap.add_argument("--no-write", action="store_true", help="do not touch the CSV")
    ap.add_argument("--no-sm", action="store_true", help="skip the ESP32 read")
    ap.add_argument("--no-light", action="store_true",
                    help="force the capture light OFF for this run")
    ap.add_argument("--light", action="store_true",
                    help="force the capture light ON for this run")
    ap.add_argument("--debug-overlay", help="write an annotated PNG here")
    ap.add_argument("--roi-file", default=ROI_FILE)
    ap.add_argument("--allow-any-day", action="store_true",
                    help=f"permit D outside 1..{CYCLE_DAYS} (testing only)")
    ap.add_argument("--force", action="store_true",
                    help="write even if this day is already in the CSV "
                         "(you almost never want this)")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    with open(args.roi_file) as f:
        cfg = json.load(f)
    rois = cfg.get("rois", cfg)                    # tolerate the flat v3 layout
    circular = bool(cfg.get("circular", True))

    d = day_index(args.allow_any_day)

    # No RTC on the Pi: after a reboot without internet the clock resumes from
    # the last hourly save and can be behind. Say so in cron.log, so a wrong D or
    # timestamp can be traced later.
    try:
        synced = subprocess.run(["timedatectl", "show", "-p", "NTPSynchronized", "--value"],
                                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5).stdout.strip()
        if synced == "no":
            print(f"[capture_gpr] WARNING: system clock not internet-synced since boot "
                  f"(now {datetime.now():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}, D={d}); time/D may be off",
                  file=sys.stderr)
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        pass

    # refuse BEFORE doing the expensive work, unless we are not writing anyway
    if not args.no_write and not args.force and already_written(d):
        sys.exit(f"[capture_gpr] day {d} is already in {OUT_CSV} -- refusing to "
                 f"duplicate.\n  Duplicated plant-days corrupt the LOOCV result. "
                 f"If today's rows are genuinely bad, delete them by hand and "
                 f"re-run, or pass --force deliberately.")

    if args.test_image:
        frame = cv2.imread(args.test_image)
        if frame is None:
            sys.exit(f"could not read {args.test_image}")
        print(f"[capture_gpr] TEST MODE on {args.test_image} "
              f"{frame.shape[1]}x{frame.shape[0]}")
    else:
        use_light = (USE_CAPTURE_LIGHT or args.light) and not args.no_light
        frame = capture_frame(use_light=use_light)

    problems = validate_rois(rois, frame.shape[:2], circular)
    if problems:
        for p in problems:
            print(f"[capture_gpr] ROI ERROR: {p}", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit("refusing to run on invalid ROIs -- fix pot_rois.json "
                 "(see make_rois.py)")

    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(frame, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
    green_all = green_mask(hsv)          # computed once, not nine times
    green_raw = hsv_mask(hsv)            # diagnostic: same thing, no opening

    # --- soil moisture, via the daemon so the serial port has one owner ---
    sm_values = None
    if not args.no_sm:
        sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
        from pi_link import get_sm_snapshot        # lazy: needs pyserial
        sm_values = get_sm_snapshot()
    if sm_values is None or len(sm_values) != 9:
        if sm_values is not None:
            print(f"[capture_gpr] SM snapshot had {len(sm_values)} values, "
                  "expected 9 -- writing NaN", file=sys.stderr)
        sm_values = [None] * 9

    prev = previous_gpr_all()
    stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M")
    overlay = frame.copy() if args.debug_overlay else None
    terr_r_px = (POT_PITCH_MM * PX_PER_MM) / 2.0
    cells = cell_masks(frame.shape[:2], rois)
    rows = []

    print(f"[capture_gpr] D={d}  kernel {MORPH_K}x{MORPH_K} "
          f"({MORPH_K / PX_PER_MM:.1f} mm)  territory r={terr_r_px:.0f} px")

    for i in range(9):
        pot_id = f"P-0{i + 1}"
        roi = rois[pot_id]
        cx, cy, r = roi_geometry(roi)

        area = roi_mask(frame.shape[:2], roi, circular)
        total = int(np.count_nonzero(area))
        g = masked_count(green_all, area)
        g_raw = masked_count(green_raw, area)
        gpr = g / total * 100.0 if total else 0.0
        gpr_raw = g_raw / total * 100.0 if total else 0.0
        cw, ch = canopy_bbox(green_all, area)

        # territory: the pot's Voronoi cell. Does not saturate when the canopy
        # outgrows the rim, which happens around D=24-28.
        t_area = circle_mask(frame.shape[:2], cx, cy, terr_r_px)
        t_total = int(np.count_nonzero(t_area))
        t_green = masked_count(green_all, t_area)
        t_gpr = t_green / t_total * 100.0 if t_total else 0.0

        # pot-area (cell) GPR -- the main growth measure from run 2 on
        c_total = int(np.count_nonzero(cells[pot_id]))
        c_green = masked_count(green_all, cells[pot_id])
        c_gpr = c_green / c_total * 100.0 if c_total else 0.0

        # anomaly flag, scaled by the gap since this pot was last recorded
        flag = 0
        if pot_id in prev:
            pday, pgpr = prev[pot_id]
            gap = max(1, d - pday)
            if abs(gpr - pgpr) > ANOMALY_DELTA_PER_DAY * gap:
                flag = 1

        sm = sm_values[i]
        sm_str = "NaN" if sm is None else f"{sm:.1f}"

        # smooth_gpr is intentionally blank. The 3-day centred moving average
        # needs days either side of each point, so it is computed offline after
        # harvest, not here (Appendix G closing note).
        rows.append([d, stamp, pot_id, sm_str, f"{gpr:.2f}", "",
                     g, total, flag,
                     f"{gpr_raw:.2f}", f"{t_gpr:.2f}", t_green, t_total,
                     cw, ch, MORPH_K,
                     f"{c_gpr:.2f}", c_green, c_total])

        print(f"{pot_id}  SM={sm_str:>6}  cell={c_gpr:6.2f}%  GPR={gpr:6.2f}%  "
              f"(noopen {gpr_raw:5.2f}%, terr {t_gpr:5.2f}%)  "
              f"({g}/{total} px)  bbox {cw}x{ch}  flag={flag}")

        if overlay is not None:
            sub = cv2.bitwise_and(green_all, green_all, mask=area)
            overlay[sub > 0] = (0, 0, 255)
            cv2.circle(overlay, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(terr_r_px), (120, 120, 120), 1)
            if circular:
                cv2.circle(overlay, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(r), (255, 255, 0), 2)
            else:
                cv2.rectangle(overlay, (roi["x"], roi["y"]),
                              (roi["x"] + roi["w"], roi["y"] + roi["h"]),
                              (255, 255, 0), 2)
            cv2.putText(overlay, f"{pot_id} {gpr:.1f}%",
                        (int(cx - r), int(cy - r) - 8),
                        cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.8, (255, 255, 0), 2)

    if overlay is not None:
        cv2.imwrite(args.debug_overlay, overlay)
        print(f"[capture_gpr] overlay -> {args.debug_overlay}")

    if args.no_write:
        print("[capture_gpr] --no-write: CSV untouched")
        return

    new_file = not os.path.exists(OUT_CSV)
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(OUT_CSV), exist_ok=True)
    with open(OUT_CSV, "a", newline="") as f:
        w = csv.writer(f)
        if new_file:
            w.writerow(CSV_HEADER)
        w.writerows(rows)

    print(f"[capture_gpr] day {d} written to {OUT_CSV}")

    # Keep the raw frame (lossless, so HSV is unchanged) for recomputation. The
    # rows are already written; a failed save must not cost the day.
    if not args.test_image:
        try:
            os.makedirs(FRAME_DIR, exist_ok=True)
            fp = os.path.join(FRAME_DIR, f"D{d:02d}_{datetime.now():%Y-%m-%d_%H%M}.png")
            if cv2.imwrite(fp, frame):
                print(f"[capture_gpr] frame -> {fp}")
            else:
                print(f"[capture_gpr] WARNING: could not save frame to {fp}", file=sys.stderr)
        except OSError as e:
            print(f"[capture_gpr] WARNING: frame not saved ({e})", file=sys.stderr)
        try:      # daily check picture: magenta = counted green, yellow = each pot's cell
            ov = frame.copy()
            ov[green_all > 0] = (255, 0, 255)
            for pid, m in cells.items():
                cnt, _ = cv2.findContours(m, cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE)
                cv2.drawContours(ov, cnt, -1, (0, 255, 255), 2)
                cx, cy, _ = roi_geometry(rois[pid])
                cv2.putText(ov, pid, (int(cx) - 30, int(cy) - 60),
                            cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.9, (0, 255, 255), 2)
            od = os.path.join(FRAME_DIR, "overlay")
            os.makedirs(od, exist_ok=True)
            cv2.imwrite(os.path.join(od, f"D{d:02d}_overlay.jpg"), ov,
                        [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 85])
        except Exception as e:      # never cost the day for a check picture
            print(f"[capture_gpr] WARNING: overlay not saved ({e})", file=sys.stderr)
    if LAST_EXPOSURE:
        new_log = not os.path.exists(EXPOSURE_LOG)
        with open(EXPOSURE_LOG, "a", newline="") as f:
            w = csv.writer(f)
            if new_log:
                w.writerow(["day", "timestamp", "exposure_us", "mean_v", "target_v",
                            "light_used"])
            w.writerow([d, stamp, LAST_EXPOSURE["exposure_us"], LAST_EXPOSURE["mean_v"],
                        TARGET_MEAN_V, LAST_EXPOSURE.get("light_used", 0)])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\live_feed.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""live_feed.py -- live ROI alignment stream for arranging the pots.

Open http://groupb.local:5000 (or http://<pi-ip>:5000) in a browser and move
each pot until its rim sits inside its green circle.

The camera is opened with capture_gpr.open_locked_camera(), i.e. the SAME
still configuration, fixed focus (LensPosition 0.42), locked exposure and white
balance as the daily GPR capture, and the circles come from the same
pot_rois.json through the same roi_geometry(). What you line up here is what
gets measured.

    python3 live_feed.py              1 fps (light on the Pi's power supply)
    python3 live_feed.py --fps 3
    python3 live_feed.py --light      capture light ON while streaming
                                      (re-sent every 15 s; goes off by itself
                                       within 20 s after you quit)

Stop with Ctrl+C. The camera can only be used by one program at a time, so
stop this before the 12:00 capture (or a bench test) needs it.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import threading
import time

import cv2
from flask import Flask, Response

from capture_gpr import ROI_FILE, open_locked_camera, roi_geometry

app = Flask(__name__)
lock = threading.Lock()
latest = {"jpeg": None}


def load_rois():
    with open(ROI_FILE) as f:
        cfg = json.load(f)
    return cfg.get("rois", cfg)


def draw(frame, rois):
    for pot_id, roi in rois.items():
        cx, cy, r = roi_geometry(roi)
        c = (int(cx), int(cy))
        cv2.circle(frame, c, int(r), (0, 255, 0), 2)          # measured area
        cv2.circle(frame, c, 3, (0, 255, 0), -1)               # centre pip
        cv2.putText(frame, pot_id, (int(cx - r), max(25, int(cy - r) - 8)),
                    cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, 0.8, (0, 255, 0), 2)
    return frame


def camera_loop(fps, use_light):
    """One camera owner; every browser tab gets the latest frame."""
    import pi_link
    rois = load_rois()
    cam = open_locked_camera()
    time.sleep(1)
    period = 1.0 / fps
    next_light = 0.0
    try:
        while True:
            t0 = time.time()
            if use_light and t0 >= next_light:
                pi_link.light_pulse(20000)            # self-expiring, refresh before it lapses
                next_light = t0 + 15
            # same colour conversion as capture_gpr.capture_frame()
            frame = cv2.cvtColor(cam.capture_array(), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
            ok, jpeg = cv2.imencode(".jpg", draw(frame, rois), [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 75])
            if ok:
                with lock:
                    latest["jpeg"] = jpeg.tobytes()
            time.sleep(max(0.0, period - (time.time() - t0)))
    finally:
        cam.stop()
        cam.close()
        if use_light:
            pi_link.set_light_state(False)


def stream(fps):
    period = 1.0 / fps
    while True:
        with lock:
            jpeg = latest["jpeg"]
        if jpeg:
            yield b"--frame\r\nContent-Type: image/jpeg\r\n\r\n" + jpeg + b"\r\n"
        time.sleep(period)


@app.route("/")
def index():
    return """
    <html>
      <body style='background-color: #121212; color: #fff; text-align: center; font-family: sans-serif; margin-top: 20px;'>
        <h2>Live ROI Alignment Guide</h2>
        <p style='color: #aaa;'>Move each physical pot until its inner rim sits squarely inside the green circle.</p>
        <img src='/video_feed' style='border: 2px solid #333; max-width: 90vw; max-height: 80vh;'>
      </body>
    </html>
    """


@app.route("/video_feed")
def video_feed():
    return Response(stream(app.config["FPS"]), mimetype="multipart/x-mixed-replace; boundary=frame")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Live ROI alignment stream")
    ap.add_argument("--fps", type=float, default=1.0, help="frames per second (default 1)")
    ap.add_argument("--light", action="store_true", help="keep the capture light on while streaming")
    ap.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=5000)
    args = ap.parse_args()
    app.config["FPS"] = max(0.5, min(args.fps, 10.0))
    threading.Thread(target=camera_loop, args=(app.config["FPS"], args.light), daemon=True).start()
    print(f"[live_feed] http://groupb.local:{args.port}  ({app.config['FPS']:g} fps, Ctrl+C to stop)")
    app.run(host="0.0.0.0", port=args.port, threaded=True)


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\make_rois.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
make_rois.py -- generate pot_rois.json for the 3x3 Pechay array.

The ROI file shipped with v3 was a placeholder and was not physically possible:
its x-pitch was 700 px and its y-pitch 390 px, which cannot both be true for a
square array viewed from nadir, and its 520x360 px boxes worked out to roughly
396 x 280 mm against a 200 mm pot. Every GPR computed from it would have been
wrong. This tool replaces guesswork with either measured pixels or geometry.

TWO MODES
---------
1. --from-centers  (PREFERRED, use once the rig is standing)
   Capture one frame with all nine pots in place, open it in any image viewer,
   read off the pixel coordinates of the centre of the top-left pot and the
   centre of the bottom-right pot, and measure one pot's rim diameter in pixels.
   Everything else is interpolated. This absorbs real lens distortion, a
   slightly off-square bench and a camera that is not perfectly centred.

       python3 make_rois.py --from-centers --tl 610 130 --br 1690 1160 \
                            --pot-px 260 -o pot_rois.json

2. --from-geometry  (planning only, before the rig exists)
   Predicts the grid from mount height and physical dimensions. Use it to sanity
   check framing and to have a file that lets the pipeline run in test mode.

       python3 make_rois.py --from-geometry --height-mm 1350 -o pot_rois.json

GUARD MARGIN
------------
Section III.C requires the ROI to sit INSIDE the pot rim with a guard margin,
so a leaf crossing from a neighbour cannot enter this pot's count. --guard is
the fraction of the rim diameter trimmed off; 0.10 (default) means the ROI is
90 % of the rim.
"""

import argparse
import json
import math

POT_IDS = [f"P-0{i}" for i in range(1, 10)]     # row by row, RIGHT to left: top row = P-03 P-02 P-01


def from_centers(tl, br, pot_px, guard):
    x0, y0 = tl
    x1, y1 = br
    side = int(round(pot_px * (1.0 - guard)))
    rois = {}
    for r in range(3):
        for c in range(3):
            cx = round(x0 + (x1 - x0) * c / 2.0)
            cy = round(y0 + (y1 - y0) * r / 2.0)
            rois[POT_IDS[r * 3 + (2 - c)]] = {"x": int(cx - side // 2),   # c=0 is the left column
                                        "y": int(cy - side // 2),
                                        "w": side, "h": side}
    return rois


def from_geometry(height_mm, pitch_mm, pot_mm, frame, hfov, vfov, guard):
    fw, fh = frame
    cov_w = 2 * height_mm * math.tan(math.radians(hfov / 2))
    cov_h = 2 * height_mm * math.tan(math.radians(vfov / 2))
    pxmm_x, pxmm_y = fw / cov_w, fh / cov_h

    span_mm = 2 * pitch_mm                       # centre of TL pot to centre of BR
    if span_mm * pxmm_y + pot_mm * pxmm_y > fh:
        raise SystemExit(
            f"array does not fit vertically at {height_mm:.0f} mm: needs "
            f"{(span_mm + pot_mm) * pxmm_y:.0f} px of {fh} px. Raise the camera.")

    tl = (fw / 2 - span_mm * pxmm_x / 2, fh / 2 - span_mm * pxmm_y / 2)
    br = (fw / 2 + span_mm * pxmm_x / 2, fh / 2 + span_mm * pxmm_y / 2)
    pot_px = pot_mm * min(pxmm_x, pxmm_y)
    meta = {"px_per_mm_x": round(pxmm_x, 4), "px_per_mm_y": round(pxmm_y, 4),
            "coverage_mm": [round(cov_w), round(cov_h)],
            "pot_px": round(pot_px)}
    return from_centers(tl, br, pot_px, guard), meta


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--from-centers", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--tl", nargs=2, type=float, metavar=("X", "Y"))
    ap.add_argument("--br", nargs=2, type=float, metavar=("X", "Y"))
    ap.add_argument("--pot-px", type=float)

    ap.add_argument("--from-geometry", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--height-mm", type=float, default=1350.0)
    ap.add_argument("--pitch-mm", type=float, default=350.0)
    ap.add_argument("--pot-mm", type=float, default=200.0)
    ap.add_argument("--hfov", type=float, default=66.0)   # Camera Module 3 std
    ap.add_argument("--vfov", type=float, default=41.0)

    ap.add_argument("--frame", nargs=2, type=int, default=[2304, 1296])
    ap.add_argument("--guard", type=float, default=0.10)
    ap.add_argument("--circular", action="store_true", default=True)
    ap.add_argument("-o", "--out", default="pot_rois.json")
    args = ap.parse_args()

    meta = {}
    if args.from_centers:
        if not (args.tl and args.br and args.pot_px):
            raise SystemExit("--from-centers needs --tl, --br and --pot-px")
        rois = from_centers(args.tl, args.br, args.pot_px, args.guard)
        source = "measured centers"
    else:
        rois, meta = from_geometry(args.height_mm, args.pitch_mm, args.pot_mm,
                                   args.frame, args.hfov, args.vfov, args.guard)
        source = "predicted from geometry -- RE-RUN WITH --from-centers ONCE THE RIG IS UP"

    doc = {
        "_comment": "Generated by make_rois.py. Do not hand-edit.",
        "_source": source,
        "_frame": args.frame,
        "_guard_fraction": args.guard,
        "_meta": meta,
        "circular": bool(args.circular),
        "rois": rois,
    }
    with open(args.out, "w") as f:
        json.dump(doc, f, indent=2)

    print(f"wrote {args.out}  ({source})")
    for k, v in rois.items():
        print(f"  {k}  x={v['x']:5d} y={v['y']:5d}  {v['w']}x{v['h']}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


=== FILE: \Dual_Archi\pi_link.py ===

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
pi_link.py -- Raspberry Pi Zero 2W side of the ESP32-S3 serial link.  v4

Thesis: Computer Vision-Based Growth Monitoring and Yield Prediction for
        Pechay Under Differential Soil Moisture Treatments in a Greenhouse

Implements the data path Appendix G line 14 assumes ("Fetch localized
Volumetric Water Content (SM) from ESP32 baseline log") and Appendix C step 7.

Three jobs:
  1. Send TIME,<unix_epoch> on connect and hourly. The ESP32 will NOT irrigate
     until it has received this, so this process must be running.
  2. Append every SM / EVT / ERR line the ESP32 emits into CSV files.
  3. Serve moisture snapshots to the noon vision script -> Deliverable 5.

This process makes NO irrigation decisions. Per Fig. 4 and Fig. 6 the ESP32-S3
owns irrigation entirely; if this script dies, watering continues.

WHY THE REQUEST-FILE HANDSHAKE (v4)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------
Previously capture_gpr.py called get_sm_snapshot(), which opened /dev/ttyAMA0
directly. But the daemon already holds that port open. Linux allows a second
open of the same tty, so both processes read from one byte stream and each
receives a fragment -- silently corrupting the daemon's log AND returning NaN
for the noon SM reading, on the one day per plant that actually matters.

Now there is exactly one owner of the serial port. Anything that needs a
reading drops a request file; the daemon services it and writes the answer to
sm_snapshot.json. The port is also opened with exclusive=True, so a second
opener fails loudly instead of corrupting data quietly.

Pi setup (once):
    sudo raspi-config
        Interface Options -> Serial Port
            login shell over serial?       NO
            serial port hardware enabled?  YES
    echo "dtoverlay=disable-bt" | sudo tee -a /boot/firmware/config.txt
    sudo reboot
    sudo apt install python3-serial

The disable-bt overlay matters: without it /dev/serial0 is the miniUART, whose
baud rate drifts with CPU frequency and corrupts bytes under load.

Wiring (see docs/WIRING.md):
    ESP32-S3 GPIO17 (TX) -> Pi GPIO15 / physical pin 10 (RXD)
    ESP32-S3 GPIO18 (RX) <- Pi GPIO14 / physical pin  8 (TXD)
    GND <-> Pi pin 6                      <-- mandatory, not optional

Run:
    python3 pi_link.py              logger daemon (normally via systemd)
    python3 pi_link.py --snapshot   one-shot SM read, prints, exits
    python3 pi_link.py --ping       link health check
    python3 pi_link.py --status     per-pot configuration dump
    python3 pi_link.py --bands-on   end germination mode
    python3 pi_link.py --clear-faults   un-quarantine all pots after repair
    python3 pi_link.py --reset-state    new planting: wipe counts, quarantines, germ flag
"""

import csv
import json
import os
import signal
import subprocess
import sys
import time
from datetime import datetime

import serial

def _default_port():
    """Prefer the real PL011. Fall back to whatever /dev/serial0 points at.

    /dev/ttyAMA0 only exists once `dtoverlay=disable-bt` is in config.txt.
    Without it /dev/serial0 is ttyS0, the miniUART, whose baud rate is tied to
    the core clock and corrupts bytes when the CPU throttles. The fallback lets
    you bench-test before the reboot, but it is NOT fit for the grow-out --
    fix config.txt. Override with AGRISENTRY_PORT=/dev/xxx if you must."""
    env = os.environ.get("AGRISENTRY_PORT")
    if env:
        return env
    if os.path.exists("/dev/ttyAMA0"):
        return "/dev/ttyAMA0"
    if os.path.exists("/dev/serial0"):
        print("[pi_link] WARNING: /dev/ttyAMA0 missing, using /dev/serial0 "
              "(miniUART). Add 'dtoverlay=disable-bt' to /boot/firmware/config.txt "
              "and reboot before collecting real data.", file=sys.stderr)
        return "/dev/serial0"
    return "/dev/ttyAMA0"


PORT = _default_port()
BAUD = 115200
DATA_DIR = os.path.expanduser("~/agrisentry_data")

SM_CSV = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "sm_log.csv")
EVT_CSV = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "irrigation_log.csv")
ERR_CSV = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "error_log.csv")
# "pot is wetter than its band, no water needed" notices -- normal, frequent
# (every 15 min per pot), so kept out of error_log.csv, which is for real faults.
BAND_CSV = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "band_log.csv")

PID_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "pi_link.pid")
REQ_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "request_sm")
SNAP_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "sm_snapshot.json")
LIGHT_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "request_light")

SM_HEADER = ["wall_time", "esp_epoch"] + [f"P-0{i}" for i in range(1, 10)]
EVT_HEADER = ["wall_time", "esp_epoch", "pot_id", "group", "vwc_before",
              "vwc_target", "dose_ml", "pulse_ms", "event_count", "status"]
ERR_HEADER = ["wall_time", "esp_epoch", "code", "detail"]
BAND_HEADER = ["wall_time", "esp_epoch", "pot_id", "vwc"]

# A snapshot older than this is not good enough to pair with a GPR row.
SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE_S = 45 * 60


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# CSV helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def ensure_csv(path, header):
    os.makedirs(DATA_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    if not os.path.exists(path):
        with open(path, "w", newline="") as f:
            csv.writer(f).writerow(header)


def append_row(path, header, row):
    ensure_csv(path, header)
    with open(path, "a", newline="") as f:
        csv.writer(f).writerow(row)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Serial
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def open_link():
    """Exclusive open. If the daemon already holds the port this raises, which
    is the point -- a loud failure beats two readers splitting one byte stream."""
    ser = serial.Serial(PORT, BAUD, timeout=2, exclusive=True)
    time.sleep(0.3)
    ser.reset_input_buffer()
    return ser


def send_time(ser):
    cmd = f"TIME,{int(time.time())}\n"
    ser.write(cmd.encode())
    print(f"[pi_link] sent {cmd.strip()}")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Clock buddy + power log
#
# The Pi has no battery clock. The ESP32 keeps time once synced and does not go
# down with the Pi, so each one can restore the other:
#   * Pi boots without internet  -> ask the ESP32 (GET_TIME) and set the Pi clock
#   * ESP32 restarts (BOOT line) -> the Pi sends it TIME immediately
# A heartbeat written every minute marks when the Pi was last alive, so after an
# unclean shutdown the next start logs when it went down and for how long.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
HEARTBEAT_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "heartbeat")
UPS_TEST_FILE = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "ups_test_start")
POWER_CSV = os.path.join(DATA_DIR, "power_log.csv")
POWER_HEADER = ["logged_at", "event", "down_since", "back_at", "downtime_h",
                "esp_last_alive", "esp_uptime_s", "clock_source", "note"]
CLOCK_MARK = "/dev/shm/agrisentry_clock_from_esp"     # tmpfs: gone after a reboot
HEARTBEAT_S = 60


def _iso(epoch):
    return datetime.fromtimestamp(epoch).isoformat(timespec="seconds") if epoch else ""


def pi_clock_trusted():
    """True if the Pi clock is internet-synced, or was set from the ESP32 this boot."""
    if os.path.exists(CLOCK_MARK):
        return True
    if os.environ.get("AGRISENTRY_FORCE_UNTRUSTED"):      # testing only
        return False
    try:
        return subprocess.run(["timedatectl", "show", "-p", "NTPSynchronized", "--value"],
                              capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5).stdout.strip() == "yes"
    except (OSError, subprocess.SubprocessError):
        return False


def ask_esp_clock(ser, timeout=3):
    """GET_TIME -> {'epoch', 'synced', 'uptime', 'prev_alive'} or None."""
    ser.write(b"GET_TIME\n")
    deadline = time.time() + timeout
    while time.time() < deadline:
        raw = ser.readline().decode(errors="replace").strip()
        if not raw:
            continue
        if raw.startswith("CLOCK,"):
            p = raw.split(",")
            try:
                return {"epoch": int(p[1]), "synced": p[2] == "1",
                        "uptime": int(p[3]), "prev_alive": int(p[4])}
            except (ValueError, IndexError):
                return None
        print(f"  < {raw}")
        handle_line(raw)
    return None


def reconcile_clock(ser):
    """Make the Pi and ESP32 agree on the time. Returns (source, esp_clock)."""
    esp = ask_esp_clock(ser)
    if pi_clock_trusted():
        send_time(ser)
        return "pi", esp
    if esp and esp["synced"]:
        r = subprocess.run(["sudo", "-n", "date", "-s", f"@{esp['epoch']}"],
                           capture_output=True, text=True)
        if r.returncode == 0:
            with open(CLOCK_MARK, "w") as f:
                f.write(str(esp["epoch"]))
            print(f"[pi_link] Pi clock was not synced -- set from the ESP32: "
                  f"{datetime.now():%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S}")
            return "esp32", esp
        print(f"[pi_link] could not set the clock from the ESP32: {r.stderr.strip()}",
              file=sys.stderr)
    print("[pi_link] WARNING: neither the Pi nor the ESP32 has a synced clock -- "
          "using the Pi's last saved time. Connect the hotspot to fix.", file=sys.stderr)
    send_time(ser)
    return "unsynced", esp


def write_heartbeat():
    tmp = HEARTBEAT_FILE + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp, "w") as f:
        f.write(str(int(time.time())))
        f.flush()
        os.fsync(f.fileno())
    os.replace(tmp, HEARTBEAT_FILE)


def log_power(event, down_since=None, back_at=None, esp=None, source="", note=""):
    hours = f"{(back_at - down_since) / 3600:.2f}" if down_since and back_at else ""
    append_row(POWER_CSV, POWER_HEADER,
               [datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds"), event, _iso(down_since),
                _iso(back_at), hours, _iso(esp["prev_alive"]) if esp else "",
                esp["uptime"] if esp else "", source, note])
    print(f"[pi_link] power log: {event} {note}")


def check_previous_shutdown(esp, source):
    """Run once at start-up. A leftover heartbeat means the last stop was unclean."""
    try:
        with open(HEARTBEAT_FILE) as f:
            last = int(f.read().strip())
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return                               # first run, or last stop was clean
    now = int(time.time())
    down_s = now - last
    if esp is None:
        note = "ESP32 did not answer"
    elif esp["synced"] and esp["uptime"] > down_s:
        # still synced (a restarted ESP32 has no time) and up longer than the Pi
        # was down -> it never lost power
        note = "Pi-only outage: the ESP32 kept running"
    else:
        note = "ESP32 restarted too (full power loss?)"
        if esp["prev_alive"]:
            note += f"; ESP32 last alive ~{_iso(esp['prev_alive'])} (+-10 min)"
    if os.path.exists(UPS_TEST_FILE):
        try:
            with open(UPS_TEST_FILE) as f:
                start = int(f.read().strip())
            note += (f"; UPS TEST: started {_iso(start)}, last alive {_iso(last)} "
                     f"-> lasted {(last - start) / 3600:.2f} h")
            os.remove(UPS_TEST_FILE)
        except (OSError, ValueError):
            pass
    if source == "unsynced":
        note += "; back_at UNRELIABLE (no synced clock)"
    log_power("UNCLEAN_SHUTDOWN", last, now, esp, source, note)


def parse_sm(raw):
    """'SM,<epoch>,v1..v9' -> (epoch, [9 floats/None]) or None if malformed."""
    parts = [p.strip() for p in raw.split(",")]
    if parts[0] != "SM" or len(parts) != 11:
        return None
    try:
        epoch = int(parts[1])
    except ValueError:
        return None
    vals = [None if v == "NaN" else float(v) for v in parts[2:]]
    return epoch, vals


def handle_line(line):
    """Route one ESP32 line to the correct CSV."""
    now = datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds")
    parts = [p.strip() for p in line.split(",")]
    kind = parts[0]

    if kind == "SM" and len(parts) == 11:
        append_row(SM_CSV, SM_HEADER, [now] + parts[1:])
    elif kind == "EVT" and len(parts) == 10:
        append_row(EVT_CSV, EVT_HEADER, [now] + parts[1:])
    elif kind == "ERR" and len(parts) >= 4 and parts[2] == "ABOVE_BAND":
        # ERR,<epoch>,ABOVE_BAND,P-04;vwc=86.4
        pot, _, vwc = parts[3].partition(";vwc=")
        append_row(BAND_CSV, BAND_HEADER, [now, parts[1], pot, vwc])
    elif kind == "ERR" and len(parts) >= 4:
        append_row(ERR_CSV, ERR_HEADER,
                   [now, parts[1], parts[2], ",".join(parts[3:])])
    # BOOT / ACK / PONG / STAT are informational: printed, not stored.


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Daemon liveness
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def daemon_pid():
    """PID of a live daemon, or None."""
    try:
        with open(PID_FILE) as f:
            pid = int(f.read().strip())
        os.kill(pid, 0)          # signal 0 = existence check only
        return pid
    except Exception:
        return None


def write_snapshot(epoch, vals):
    tmp = SNAP_FILE + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp, "w") as f:
        json.dump({"esp_epoch": epoch,
                   "wall_time": datetime.now().isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
                   "unix": int(time.time()),
                   "values": vals}, f)
    os.replace(tmp, SNAP_FILE)   # atomic: a reader never sees a half-written file


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Snapshot API used by capture_gpr.py
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def get_sm_snapshot(timeout=25):
    """Return a list of 9 values (None for a faulted pot), or None on failure.

    Routes through the daemon when one is running, so the serial port only ever
    has a single owner. Falls back to a direct read when it is not."""
    if daemon_pid():
        return _snapshot_via_daemon(timeout)
    return _snapshot_direct(timeout)

def _snapshot_via_daemon(timeout):
    os.makedirs(DATA_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    before = os.path.getmtime(SNAP_FILE) if os.path.exists(SNAP_FILE) else 0
    with open(REQ_FILE, "w") as f:
        f.write(str(int(time.time())))

    deadline = time.time() + timeout
    while time.time() < deadline:
        if os.path.exists(SNAP_FILE) and os.path.getmtime(SNAP_FILE) > before:
            try:
                with open(SNAP_FILE) as f:
                    snap = json.load(f)
                if int(time.time()) - snap.get("unix", 0) <= SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE_S:
                    return snap["values"]
            except Exception:
                pass
            break
        time.sleep(0.5)

    print("[pi_link] daemon did not answer the snapshot request", file=sys.stderr)
    return _latest_from_log()


def _latest_from_log():
    """Last resort: newest row of sm_log.csv, if it is recent enough.

    Better than NaN, but only when it is genuinely fresh -- pairing a GPR row
    with hours-old moisture would quietly corrupt the SM column."""
    if not os.path.exists(SM_CSV):
        return None
    try:
        with open(SM_CSV, newline="") as f:
            rows = list(csv.DictReader(f))
        if not rows:
            return None
        last = rows[-1]
        age = (datetime.now() -
               datetime.fromisoformat(last["wall_time"])).total_seconds()
        if age > SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE_S:
            print(f"[pi_link] newest sm_log row is {age/60:.0f} min old -- rejecting",
                  file=sys.stderr)
            return None
        return [None if last[f"P-0{i}"] == "NaN" else float(last[f"P-0{i}"])
                for i in range(1, 10)]
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[pi_link] could not read {SM_CSV}: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
        return None


def _snapshot_direct(timeout):
    try:
        with open_link() as ser:
            send_time(ser)
            ser.write(b"GET_SM\n")
            deadline = time.time() + timeout
            while time.time() < deadline:
                raw = ser.readline().decode(errors="replace").strip()
                parsed = parse_sm(raw)
                if parsed:
                    return parsed[1]
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[pi_link] direct snapshot failed: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
    return None

def set_light_state(on: bool, timeout=5) -> bool:
    """Turn Relay 12 (Flash / Grow Light) ON or OFF.
    
    Routes through the daemon via a request file if running,
    otherwise sends the UART command directly.
    """
    if daemon_pid():
        return _set_light_via_daemon(on, timeout)
    return _set_light_direct(on)


def _set_light_via_daemon(on: bool, timeout=5) -> bool:
    os.makedirs(DATA_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    val = "ON" if on else "OFF"
    with open(LIGHT_FILE, "w") as f:
        f.write(val)

    # Wait for the daemon to process and delete the file
    deadline = time.time() + timeout
    while time.time() < deadline:
        if not os.path.exists(LIGHT_FILE):
            return True
        time.sleep(0.05)
    return False


def _set_light_direct(on: bool) -> bool:
    cmd = b"LIGHT_ON\n" if on else b"LIGHT_OFF\n"
    try:
        with open_link() as ser:
            ser.write(cmd)
            resp = ser.readline().decode(errors="replace").strip()
            return "ACK,LIGHT" in resp
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[pi_link] direct light toggle failed: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
        return False

def light_pulse(ms, timeout=5) -> bool:
    """Turn the capture light ON for ms milliseconds. The ESP32 switches it off
    by itself when the time is up, so a crashed caller can't leave it on.

    Routes through the daemon when one is running (it confirms the daemon took
    the request; the ESP32's ACK lands in pi_link.log), otherwise talks to the
    ESP32 directly and waits for its ACK."""
    if daemon_pid():
        os.makedirs(DATA_DIR, exist_ok=True)
        with open(LIGHT_FILE, "w") as f:
            f.write(f"PULSE,{int(ms)}")
        deadline = time.time() + timeout
        while time.time() < deadline:
            if not os.path.exists(LIGHT_FILE):
                return True
            time.sleep(0.05)
        return False
    try:
        with open_link() as ser:
            ser.write(f"LIGHT_PULSE,{int(ms)}\n".encode())
            deadline = time.time() + timeout
            while time.time() < deadline:
                raw = ser.readline().decode(errors="replace").strip()
                if raw.startswith("ACK,LIGHT_PULSE"):
                    return True
                if raw.startswith("ERR,"):
                    print(f"[pi_link] light pulse refused: {raw}", file=sys.stderr)
                    return False
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[pi_link] light pulse failed: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
    return False


def simple_command(cmd, expect, timeout=8):
    """One-shot command. Refuses to run while the daemon owns the port."""
    if daemon_pid():
        print("[pi_link] daemon is running and owns the serial port.\n"
              "          stop it first:  sudo systemctl stop pi-link", file=sys.stderr)
        return False
    try:
        with open_link() as ser:
            ser.write((cmd + "\n").encode())
            deadline = time.time() + timeout
            while time.time() < deadline:
                raw = ser.readline().decode(errors="replace").strip()
                if raw:
                    print(f"  < {raw}")
                if raw.startswith(expect):
                    return True
    except Exception as exc:
        print(f"[pi_link] {cmd} failed: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
    return False


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Daemon
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
def daemon():
    os.makedirs(DATA_DIR, exist_ok=True)
    for path, header in ((SM_CSV, SM_HEADER), (EVT_CSV, EVT_HEADER),
                         (ERR_CSV, ERR_HEADER)):
        ensure_csv(path, header)

    with open(PID_FILE, "w") as f:
        f.write(str(os.getpid()))

    # systemctl stop / reboot send SIGTERM: exit through `finally`, so a clean
    # stop removes the heartbeat and is not mistaken for a power loss.
